## 🚀 [Unreleased]

### Fixed

- **`preallocate_kv_blocks` is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `batch.rs` reported 32 survivors; the largest coherent group was this function, which had NO unit test at all (only an indirect integration file). Its contract is that the model's prefill writes K/V into `seq.kv_blocks`, so a table that is one block SHORT makes `write_prefill_kv` fall back to block 0 and corrupt the cache — the count must be exact, not merely non-zero. The new test builds a prompt spanning two blocks plus one token and requires exactly three distinct allocations (not repeated placeholders), then exhausts a one-block pool and requires the loop to stop without panicking or over-filling. Nine mutants die: the whole-body `()` replacement, all three relaxations of the `while len < blocks_needed` condition, and one slice-boundary arithmetic under `multi-node`. Three remain, all inside the `#[cfg(feature = "multi-node")]` block computing the token slice passed to `record_block_tokens` (`block_idx * BLOCK_SIZE`, `start + BLOCK_SIZE`); observing them needs the chain hashes, which the manager keeps private, so they stay visible rather than excluded.
- **`SchedulerEngine`'s `Debug` rendering and `schedule` are covered — `state/mod.rs` reaches zero survivors (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the file's last three mutants are gone. (1) `Debug::fmt` replaced by `Ok(Default::default())` writes NOTHING while still succeeding. `Debug` is the operator's only view into a stuck scheduler, so an empty rendering removes queue depth, running/finished counts and the id counter exactly when a hang is being diagnosed; the test requires the struct name, the count and id-counter fields, and a rendering long enough to hold ten fields. (2) `schedule()` replaced by `None` — callers read `None` as "nothing to do this round", so always returning it means admitted requests are never composed and the served model simply never runs. The test requires `None` while idle, `Some` carrying the admitted id once a request exists, and `None` again after the queue drains. Note on classification: the second `schedule` mutant (`Some(Default::default())`) had been reported UNVIABLE under the full-file scan because `Batch` has no `Default` in that build, yet under the narrow test filter it compiles and is caught by this test — an UNVIABLE verdict is not a permanent property of the mutant, so it is worth re-checking one when the surrounding test set changes. Full-file result: 31 killed, 0 survived, 5 unviable.
- **`set_policy`, `register_observer` and `running` are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the last three accessors on `state/mod.rs` (survivors 6 -> 3). (1) `set_policy` emptied to `()`, which is silent misconfiguration: a caller believes it reconfigured scheduling and the old policy keeps running. The test swaps the default for `PriorityPolicy` and requires `policy.name()` to change. (2) `register_observer` replaced by `Ok(())`, which reports success past `SchedulerObservers::MAX_OBSERVERS` (16) while silently dropping the observer. The test registers exactly 16 successfully and requires the 17th to return `Err`, so it pins the boundary from both sides rather than just "registration works". (3) `running()` replaced by `vec![]`, which reports an idle scheduler while sequences are decoding. Survivors 6 -> 3. Worth noting for the remaining three: `set_policy` now comes back as UNVIABLE rather than survived, because the test reads `self.policy` through the mutated setter and the mutant no longer compiles — an unviable verdict there is evidence the test binds the behaviour, not a gap.
- **`SchedulerEngine` bookkeeping accessors and the prefix-cache rate are covered, and the mutation harness no longer leaks processes (test coverage + tooling, vllm-core, scripts)** — `scheduler/engine/state/mod.rs` had 17 survivors, dominated by plain accessors that returned constants: `stop_ended_len`, `pending_stream_len`, `finished_sequences`, `get_sequence_mut`, and `clear_finished` emptied to `()`. These are not cosmetic — `stop_ended` and `pending_stream` are keyed by MONOTONIC sequence ids, so a leftover entry is never reused and leaks for the life of the process, and the accessors exist precisely so tests can assert that release. The new test drives each collection and requires the accessor to report it (2 entries, 1 entry, the finalized id 42, a live mutable borrow that actually writes through, `None` for an unknown id, and emptiness after `clear_finished`). `prefix_cache_hit_rate` was replaced with the constant 1.0 and its division turned into `%` and into `*` with no test comparing a value; it now must report 0.0 for zero requests and exactly 0.5 for 2 hits of 4 lookups, a fixture where a constant or a product cannot coincide. Survivors 17 -> 6. Tooling: the harness previously used `subprocess.run(..., timeout=...)`, which terminates only the shell — the `cargo test` beneath it became an orphan and kept running (four such processes were found last round, one alive for two hours). `run_test_command` now starts the child with `start_new_session=True` and kills the whole process group on timeout; verified directly by running a sleeping command under a 2-second timeout and confirming no `sleep` survived.
- **The sequence-id counter's skip and advance are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `add_request_inner` walks `next_seq_id` forward past any id already held, adopts it, then advances again; `cargo mutants` turned both `+= 1` sites into `-= 1` / `*= 1`. The new test claims a HIGH explicit id (500) that the counter never issued, then requires auto-assigned ids to be non-zero, mutually distinct, and to skip 500; it then parks the counter ON 500 and requires the walk to move FORWARD past it — plus a FOLLOW-UP allocation, which was needed because the first version passed while `next_seq_id` never advanced: only after asking for one more id does a stalled counter show up. Both `-= 1` variants now fail. The remaining `*= 1` at the advance site is proven equivalent and registered: parking the counter on the id just issued is repaired by the next admission's `while seq_id_is_taken(...)` loop, so the ids issued are identical and the only cost is one extra iteration of a bounded loop. The `+= 1` INSIDE that loop is not equivalent — `-= 1` walks backwards and `*= 1` cannot terminate — and both are killed by the same test. `docs/mutation-equivalents.md` records both halves together so the distinction between the two `+= 1` sites is not lost.
- **Duplicate sequence ids and the no-prefix-cache admission path are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `scheduler/engine/state/request.rs` had 13 survivors. Two are the id-collision contract: `seq_id_is_taken` checks BOTH `running` and the waiting queue, and `cargo mutants` replaced it with the constants `true`/`false` and flipped its `||` to `&&`. No test ever submitted two requests that collide, so the duplicate path — and the "skip ids the counter never handed out" loop beside it — was never entered; a collision slipping through means the second sequence overwrites the first and the caller gets back an id naming different work than it asked for. The new test occupies id 5 through the QUEUE and separately through `running`, requiring a different id each time while both requests stay present. The third is `add_request_without_prefix_cache` replaced by `Default::default()` — it returned 0, which is not a valid sequence id — surviving because existing tests only call plain `add_request`; it is the path stateful models take so a full prefix hit cannot admit a sequence into Decode with fresh `None` recurrent state. Survivors 13 -> 10. Left visible rather than excluded: `lookup_distributed_matched_tokens` returns a constant in the not-`multi-node` stub; the value only gates a `trace!` and fills an `ObserverEvent`, both unobservable under the default `NoopSchedulerObserver`, so a test would have to install a custom observer to see it.
- **`requeue_stuck_prefills` is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `scheduler/engine/state/batch.rs` had 40 survivors, and the most consequential was `requeue_stuck_prefills` replaced wholesale by `()`. Its contract is that a sequence left in `running` while still `Prefilling` or `Waiting` is NEVER re-scheduled (running is only re-included when `status == Decoding`), so it stalls forever while pinning its pre-allocated KV blocks. Nothing called it with an orphaned sequence in place. The new test injects one healthy decoder and two orphans directly into `running` (bypassing `update`, which is exactly why they are orphans), then requires that only the decoder survives and both orphans appear in the waiting queue rather than vanishing. Eight of the nine mutants now fail. The ninth (`i += 1` relaxed to `i *= 1`) is worth recording because it argues for the test rather than against it: hand-applying it HANGS the test — the index never advances, so the loop never terminates. The verification harness reports such a mutant as `SURVIVED` because its per-mutant timeout is classified as "did not fail", which is a false survivor caused by an infinite loop, not a gap. Process note for future rounds: this file's scan takes over ten minutes and one run left an orphaned `cargo test` alive for two hours before it was noticed; long mutation scans here need their job ids tracked and the process table checked afterwards.
- **`RequestQueue::drain_by_phase` compacts its priority heap (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `request_queue.rs` had a single survivor: `cleanup_priority_queue` replaced by `()`. It is NOT a correctness bug — `dequeue` already skips stale heap entries by testing membership in `sequences` — it is an unbounded-growth bug: every drained sequence would leave one dead slot in the heap for the life of the process. The heap is private, so the test reads it from the sibling test module (which can see ancestor privates) and asserts the heap SHRINKS from two entries to one when the Decode phase is drained, alongside the sequence map agreeing. Survivors 1 -> 0 for the file. This round also CORRECTS a standing claim I had been repeating: `RequestQueue::get_mut` is not unused — it has a test at `request_queue/tests.rs:140`, and the mutant "replace `get_mut` with `None`" is killed by it. The open question about it was based on a stale reading and should be dropped.
- **`InMemoryMetricsExporter` is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `exporter/mod.rs` had 9 survivors, all on the standard test double: `record` emptied to `()`, `snapshot` replaced with eight different fixed vectors, and `export` turned into `Ok(String::new())`. Every variant survived because all three existing tests in that module exercise the OTHER types — `PrometheusExporter` and the `default_arc` stub — so the in-memory exporter was never called. One test now covers its whole contract: a fresh exporter is empty; two names record; re-recording an existing name OVERWRITES rather than duplicates (which `snapshot` returning a fixed vector cannot satisfy); `snapshot` reports the latest values after sorting; and `export` renders one `name value` line per metric with the latest value. Survivors 9 -> 0. Also investigated `engine/paged_kv_cache.rs` and found its one reported survivor is a DEAD arm rather than a coverage gap: the `#[cfg(not(feature = "multi-node"))]` stub references `vllm_dist::BlockDataSource`, but `vllm-dist` is an OPTIONAL dependency enabled only by the `multi-node` feature, so that arm cannot be compiled in any configuration. Proof: injecting a deliberate syntax error into the stub body left `cargo check -p vllm-core --lib` at zero errors, so the branch is discarded outright. Reported rather than patched, since changing a feature-gated public accessor is outside a test-coverage round.
- **`MemoryManager`'s post-construction setters are covered, and the exclusion checker had a path bug (test coverage + tooling, vllm-core, scripts)** — re-running the `scheduler/memory/mod.rs` scan under `--features multi-node` cut survivors from 18 to 4, which revealed that most of the previously-reported "gaps" were `#[cfg(feature = "multi-node")]` code that does not compile on default features (4 more are unviable). Two genuine survivors were the POST-CONSTRUCTION setters: `set_distributed_kv` and `set_block_hasher` replaced by `()` passed, because every existing test uses the builder forms `with_distributed_kv` / `with_block_hasher` and neither setter is the builder. New tests exercise the setter path specifically — allocation must not touch the cache before `set_distributed_kv` and must register two updates after it; and `set_block_hasher` must install a DIFFERENT hasher, asserted by comparing `name()` before and after (the first version compared against `XorShiftHasher`, which turned out to be the default, so it asserted nothing — `IdentityHasher` provides the contrast). Survivors 4 -> 2. Separately, `scripts/check_mutant_exclusions.py` reported two valid new exclusions as STALE: for a bare basename like `mod.rs` it picked `sorted(rglob(basename))[0]`, which is `crates/core/src/circuit_breaker/mod.rs` — 69 files share that name — so it listed the wrong module's mutants. The checker now lists EVERY same-named file and unions the results, which also protects any future nested-module exclusion.
- **`MemoryManager::execute_preemption` is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the manager-level preemption loop had FIVE survivors, including "replace the whole function with `vec![]`", because only the ENGINE-level `SchedulerEngine::execute_preemption` had a test and the two are different functions. The new test builds three sequences (two decoders at 9 and 3 rounds, one Prefilling) holding two blocks each, demands exactly one sequence's worth, and pins the four things the loop must get right: only `Status::Decoding` is eligible (the Prefilling sequence must survive however many blocks it holds), the sequence with the MOST decode rounds is chosen first, a satisfied demand stops the loop after one victim rather than draining every candidate, and the preempted sequence is both removed from `running` and has its blocks returned to the pool. Manager survivors 26 -> 18. Remaining are the distributed/`multi-node` wiring pairs (`with_distributed_kv`, `set_distributed_kv`, `record_block_tokens`, `lookup_distributed_prefix`), which compile only under that feature, plus `rollback`'s boundary.
- **`MemoryManager`'s pool accessors are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — scanning `scheduler/memory/mod.rs` left 26 survivors, the largest single-file gap found so far. The first batch is the accessors: `total_blocks` replaced by the constants 0 and 1, and `get_block_ref_count` by the constant 1, all survived because the existing tests use `available_blocks` (well covered) but never its siblings. A wrong `total_blocks` is not cosmetic — every usage ratio an operator reads is `used / total`, so a constant would report a fixed percentage regardless of the real pool. The new test asserts the configured pool size, the refcount of a recorded block, and zero for an unknown block, then releases and requires the pool to be whole again. Survivors 26 -> 23. The remaining 23 are dominated by the `distributed_kv` / `block_hasher` / `block_data_source` wiring (`with_*` / `set_*` / getter pairs), which are `multi-node`-adjacent constructors, plus `execute_preemption`'s accounting and `record_block_tokens`; those need a manager built with those components attached, which the default-feature test fixture does not provide.
- **Eviction policy survivors reach zero (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the two remaining survivors from the previous round needed fixtures the existing tests could not express. (1) `compute_priority` flips a long-running decode sequence from priority 3 to 1 at `consecutive_decode_rounds > 5`; `cargo mutants` relaxed that to `>=`, moving the boundary one round early, and nothing inspected the boundary itself. The new test pins 5 rounds at priority 3 and 6 rounds at priority 1, and additionally pins the `Prefilling` arm (flat 2) and the excluded-status arm (0) so a deleted match branch cannot slip through either. (2) `release_blocks` removes a freed block from `block_access_order` with `retain(|&b| b != block)`; flipping `!=` to `==` keeps ONLY the freed block and prunes the rest. The existing final-reference test tracks a SINGLE block, where both forms coincide. The new test tracks `[1, 2, 3]`, releases the middle one, and requires the list to become `[3, 1]`; it then releases the survivors to prove the bookkeeping stayed consistent. `eviction.rs` now reports zero behavioural survivors (two mutants are unviable and cannot compile).
- **Eviction policy: the status filter and the LRU reordering are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — scanning `scheduler/memory/eviction.rs` left 5 survivors (plus 2 unviable). (1) `select_victims` skips `Status::Finished || Status::Waiting`; `cargo mutants` turned that `||` into `&&`, which can never be true because a sequence holds exactly one status — so NOTHING is skipped and finished sequences compete to become eviction victims, potentially reclaiming a block still owned by a live sequence that co-owns it at a lower priority. The new test excludes a finished and a waiting sequence, with a decoding-sequence control proving the policy does still return victims for eligible input. (2) `record_blocks`, `release_blocks` and `touch_blocks` all do `block_access_order.retain(|&b| b != block)` then push the block to the front. Flipping `!=` to `==` keeps ONLY the touched block and discards every other entry, collapsing the LRU list to one item. The existing test asserted only that the FRONT is the touched block, which the broken version also satisfies; the new test asserts the whole sequence (`[3,2,1]` -> touch 1 -> `[1,3,2]`, and re-recording 2 -> `[2,1,3]`). Survivors 5 -> 2. Remaining: `compute_priority`'s `> 5` relaxed to `>=`, and the same `!=` in `release_blocks`, both needing a fixture where the freed block is NOT the last reference.
- **`BlockAllocator::free`'s range guard is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — scanning `scheduler/memory` (119 mutants) left 27 survivors, and the allocator's `free` path was one of them: the guard `if block < self.num_blocks as BlockId` protects the `is_free[block]` index, and `cargo mutants` relaxed it to `<=`, which reads one slot past the end. Nothing fed `free` a bad id, so the mutation was invisible. One test now covers the whole function's contract: an id equal to `num_blocks` must be ignored, a wildly out-of-range id (`u32::MAX`) must be ignored without panicking, and freeing an already-free block must count once — the last two exercise the neighbouring `warn!` branch that exists precisely because a corrupted id reaching here is what the guard is for. `allocator.rs` survivors went to ZERO. Note for future rounds: running mutants here regenerates `proptest-regressions/scheduler/memory/allocator/` (seeds written while mutants were applied); both recorded cases pass on committed code, so the directory is an artefact and was deleted rather than checked in.
- **OTLP config validation and `updown_delta`'s i64 clamp are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — continuing the `otlp.rs` sweep (survivors 41 -> 21). (1) `OtlpConfig::validate` checks three fields, but the tests only exercised an out-of-range ratio and a zero interval; `cargo mutants` could delete the `!` in `endpoint.trim().is_empty()`, flip both `||` operands of the ratio guard and invert the interval `== 0`, all surviving. The missing cases are the ones a hand-written config actually hits: an EMPTY endpoint, a WHITESPACE-ONLY endpoint, and a NaN ratio — the last is why `is_finite()` exists at all, since every comparison with NaN is false and the range check alone would accept it. The new test rejects all three plus infinity, and requires the boundary values 0.0 and 1.0 to be ACCEPTED, so it cannot pass by rejecting everything. (2) `updown_delta` narrows an i128 difference to i64 and clamps with `if delta < 0 { i64::MIN } else { i64::MAX }`; the existing test used only small values where the narrowing never fails. Driving it from `u64::MAX` to 0 and back forces both clamp arms: the mutants that inverted the comparison now return the wrong bound. One variant (`<` to `<=`) stays equivalent — at `delta == 0` both arms yield `i64::MAX`.
- **The OTLP exporter's snapshot accessors are covered, and its tests had never been compiled (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `metrics/exporter/otlp.rs` (980 lines, 59 mutants) first reported ALL 59 as surviving, including mutants that replace `updown_delta` wholesale even though a unit test asserting `updown_delta(None, 5) == 5` exists. The cause is a feature gate: `exporter/mod.rs` declares `#[cfg(feature = "opentelemetry")] pub(crate) mod otlp;`, so on default features neither the module nor its 13 tests are compiled — and `cargo test --lib metrics` was running 45 tests, not 58. Re-verifying with `--features opentelemetry` dropped survivors to 41 immediately. The real gap was then `snapshot_f64` / `snapshot_u64`: two long `match`es mapping Prometheus wire names to `MetricsSnapshot` fields, called by every exported gauge and counter yet never tested directly — deleting an arm or returning a constant left `/metrics` missing a series with nothing failing. `snapshot_accessors_map_each_wire_name` builds a snapshot where every field holds a distinct value and asserts each name maps to its own field, including the unknown-name fallbacks; 18 mutants now fail. Also added a test pinning `impl Default`'s documented values (endpoint `http://localhost:4317`, service name, crate version, 30s interval, 1.0 sampling ratio). Recorded rather than forced: the per-field `#[serde(default = "OtlpConfig::default_*")]` hooks are a SECOND, hand-duplicated source of the same defaults that `impl Default` does not call, and reaching them needs a deserialisation test — `serde_json` is not a dependency of `vllm-core`, and adding one for a test is out of scope, so those mutants stay visible rather than being excluded. Survivors 59 -> 41.
- **The Prometheus exporter's public surface and gauge scaling are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `prometheus.rs` had 6 survivors, all on the type's actual job. `port()` replaced by the constants 0 and 1, and `export()` replaced by an empty string and by `"xyzzy"`, all passed: the three existing tests never call either, even though `export()` is the entire reason the type exists. One test now drives a configured port (9411, chosen so no constant or default coincides) and requires the body to be non-empty and to carry the collector's recorded counter. The other gap was fixed-point scaling: both `speculative_acceptance_rate` and `throughput_speedup_ratio` are stored scaled by 100000 and divided back out on export, and `cargo mutants` turned the SECOND division into `%` and `*`; only the acceptance-rate line had a value assertion, so `throughput_speedup_ratio` was unchecked. Recording 1.5 now must render `throughput_speedup_ratio 1.500` and must not leak the raw `150000000`. A first version of the port test also asserted the port appears in the exported TEXT, which it does not — the port is for listening, not content — and that wrong assumption was removed rather than worked around. Survivors for the file: 6 -> 0.
- **`EnhancedMetricsCollector`'s recorders are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `collector/sampler/runtime.rs` had 13 survivors, and these were not subtle: emptying `record_batch_size`, `record_scheduler_wait_time`, `record_prefix_cache_hit`, `record_prefix_cache_request`, `record_cuda_graph_miss`, `set_queue_depth` or `set_active_sequences` outright all passed, because the existing tests assert on OTHER recorders or read the wrong collector type. That last part matters: `LockFreeMetrics::record_batch_size` and `EnhancedMetricsCollector::record_batch_size` are different methods, and a test written against the former does not exercise the latter — a distinction my first attempt at this file got wrong. Thirty-third-round tests now drive each recorder and read the value back through the getter the exporter uses (`set_queue_depth` -> `request_queue_depth` gauge, `record_cuda_graph_miss` -> `cuda_graph_misses_total`, the prefix-cache pair -> both the direct getters and the hit-rate on the snapshot, batch size -> `avg_batch_size`, wait time -> `avg_scheduler_wait_time_ms`). Two fixtures had to be sharpened rather than written once: a getter returning the constant `1` was indistinguishable from a correct getter because the test recorded exactly one hit, so the counts were made to differ (2 hits of 3 requests); the same trap in the earlier `lock_free` round produced a false survivor that survived one extra verification pass. Survivors 13 -> 2, and the remaining two (`if prefill > 0` / `if decode > 0` relaxed to `>=`) are equivalent — adding zero tokens to an `AtomicU64` changes neither the counter nor the derived throughput — now registered in `docs/mutation-equivalents.md`.
- **A `lock_free` survivor was a clamp, not a gap — and a column-number trap nearly hid that (mutation classification, vllm-core)** — `metrics/lock_free.rs` still reported `239:89: replace - with +` as a survivor after the previous round's percentile work. Chasing it exposed a trap worth recording: that ONE line contains two minus signs — column 50 inside `(p * (xs.len() as f64 - 1.0))`, which sets the percentile RANK, and column 89 inside `min(xs.len() - 1)`, which is the saturation clamp. `cargo mutants` reports the second; my earlier verification had been reasoning about the first. Testing them separately settles it: column 50 is a genuine gap and is already killed by `test_snapshot_percentiles_match_the_index_formula` (mutating it yields p50 = 51 instead of 50), while column 89 is structurally equivalent — the index is `floor(p * (n - 1))`, which for `p` in `0..=1` never exceeds `n - 1`, so the `min` never binds and both `n - 1` and `n + 1` return the index unchanged. Registered with its reasoning in `docs/mutation-equivalents.md`, including the warning that a verification run trusting a stale column will test the wrong operator and report a false survivor. Also added a value test for `avg_batch_size` (mean of `[2, 4, 6]` is 4.0, current is the last sample) and for the prefix cache rate at a non-trivial fraction (3 hits of 4 requests is 75%, not the trivially-100% the existing fixture produced), which is what killed the corresponding division mutants. Process note: one hand-applied mutant was left un-restored for a few steps and produced two red tests; caught by running the suite immediately after, restored, and re-verified green before committing.
- **`lock_free`'s latency percentiles and throughput aggregates are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — a first scan of `metrics/` (235 mutants) started with `lock_free.rs` (59), which had 13 genuine survivors despite the file being read by every metrics test. Two gaps explain them. (1) The percentiles are `xs[floor(p * (n - 1))]` over the sorted window, and no test had ever compared them against a value: mutating the `- 1` to `+` or `/`, or the index multiplication, went unnoticed. `test_snapshot_percentiles_match_the_index_formula` pins samples `1..=100` where every rank is unambiguous (p50 = 50, p90 = 90, p99 = 99, mean = 50.5). (2) `prefill_throughput` / `decode_throughput` divide phase tokens by `uptime` behind a guard, and nothing recorded phase tokens then read the rate back; `test_snapshot_throughput_is_positive_after_recording_phase_tokens` requires both to be positive, decode strictly below prefill when it recorded half the tokens, and an idle collector to report exactly 0 rather than NaN. Survivors went 13 -> 11. Recorded for the next round: the remaining ones are the `avg_batch_size` and `prefix_cache_hit_rate` divisions plus the percentile index — the `lock_free/tests.rs` suite already asserts the KV/hit-rate values through a different path, so those need targeted value checks rather than the broad aggregate test I first wrote (which duplicated existing coverage and was replaced). Also note: this module's mutants make the test harness abort rather than fail, leaving ~320 `crates/core/core.*` dumps that must be removed before committing.
- **The `ha` module — leader election and failover — is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — a first scan of `ha/` left 14 survivors across its two files, which together had only six tests. The most important gap was `LeaderElection::is_leader_or_promote`, the entry point every caller uses before doing leader-only work: it had NO test at all, so replacing the whole function with `true` or `false`, or damaging the `while start.elapsed() < timeout` window, went unnoticed. Three tests now pin its arms — an established leader returns true promptly (asserted as wall-clock, so a mutant that sleeps the retry interval fails), an absent leader is claimed and the state actually changes, and a live leader held by ANOTHER node is not stolen and the call times out rather than returning early. For `FailoverManager`, three more: `get_stale_requests` must both REPORT and RELEASE timed-out requests (with a long-timeout control that keeps a fresh request in flight), `migrate_request` must distinguish a hit from a miss, and `handle_leader_transfer` must demote the old leader and record the new one. Also caught a ghost-state bug: `leader_election()` returning `Arc::new(Default::default())` survived, which would hand callers a permanent "no leader" view while the manager's real state moved on — the new test takes leadership through the accessor and requires the manager's own field to see it. Two boundary relaxations are proven equivalent and registered: `duration_since(created_at) > request_timeout` with `>=` needs a nanosecond-exact Duration equality from two independent `Instant` reads, and `elapsed() < timeout` with `<=` differs only at `timeout == 0`, where `elapsed()` is already non-zero (measured 121ns immediately after `Instant::now()`).
- **`sample_batch`'s repeat-penalty guard is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the guard is `(repeat_penalty - 1.0).abs() > EPSILON && !seen.is_empty()`, and seven operator mutants of that one line survived because no test drove `sample_batch` through the penalty at all (the previous round's temperature work left `top_k > 0` covered but not this). Using `T = 0` makes each arm a deterministic greedy pick, so the three cases separate cleanly: a penalty of 8.0 on the repeated argmax demotes logit 3.0 to 0.375, below token 2's 1.0, and the pick moves from token 1 to token 2; a neutral penalty of 1.0 leaves the argmax; an empty history disables the penalty however large it is. Probed all three first, then wrote the assertions. The `!` deletion — which inverts the history test so a penalty applies only when there is NO history — now fails. Four variants were proven equivalent and registered in `docs/mutation-equivalents.md`: `-` to `+`/`/` makes the guard true at `penalty == 1.0`, where `apply_repeat_penalty` divides by 1.0 (for every other penalty the original guard was already true); `>` to `>=` differs only at `|penalty - 1| == f32::EPSILON`; and `&&` to `||` opens two paths that are both no-ops — an empty history iterates nothing, and `penalty == 1.0` divides by 1.0.
- **`sample_batch`'s temperature scaling is covered, and `T = 0` was silently NaN-poisoning the row (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the guard is `temperature > 0.0 && (temperature - 1.0).abs() > EPSILON { *l /= temperature }`, and six of its operator mutants survived because existing tests only assert that some token is produced. The load-bearing one is `temperature > 0.0` relaxed to `>=`: it lets `T = 0` reach `*l /= temperature`, and a logit of exactly `0.0` divides to `0.0 / 0.0 = NaN` rather than `inf`. The dispatch below still sends `T = 0` to `greedy_sample`, but it now runs on a NaN-poisoned row where the argmax is whatever the comparison happens to favour — measured: `[2.0, 0.0, 0.0] / 0.0` yields `[inf, NaN, NaN]`. `sample_batch_greedy_at_zero_temperature_without_scaling` pins the argmax on two rows, the second with the argmax NOT first so a positional accident cannot make it pass. `sample_batch_applies_temperature_scaling` covers the scaling itself with a three-point comparison (`T = 0.5` sharpens so the argmax takes >= 150/200; `T = 1.0` keeps the tail reachable as the control; `T = 2.0` flattens by >= 0.1 share). Three equivalences were proven rather than papered over and are registered in `docs/mutation-equivalents.md`: replacing `-` with `+` or `/` in `(temperature - 1.0).abs()` makes the guard true at `T == 1.0`, where the division is by 1.0 and changes nothing (for every other T the original guard was already true); and `>` -> `>=` differs only when `|T - 1|` is exactly `f32::EPSILON`, a temperature within one f32 ulp of 1.0.
- **`sample_batch`'s nucleus dispatch and top-k mask are covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the legacy scalar entry point shares logic with `sample_one_with_params` but had its own untested arms (21 survivors). (1) The dispatch is `temperature <= 0.0` -> greedy, else `top_p < 1.0` -> `top_p_sample`, else `temperature_sample`; `cargo mutants` flipped `top_p < 1.0` to `==`, `>` and `<=` and all three survived, because existing tests only assert that *some* token comes back. The consequence is visible: with logits `[1, 0, -1]` and `top_p = 0.5` the nucleus is token 0 alone, so any other token means the sampler used the untruncated distribution. `sample_batch_respects_the_nucleus` draws 200 times and requires token 0 every time, plus a `top_p = 1.0` control proving the tail IS reachable, so the assertion is not vacuous. (2) The top-k mask `if *l < threshold` decides which logits become `-inf`; flipping it to `==`, `>` or `<=` survived for the same reason. `sample_batch_top_k_masks_below_the_threshold` requires `top_k = 1` to leave only the argmax reachable across 200 draws, with a `top_k = 2` control that the second-largest becomes reachable. Writing that control caught a wrong assumption of mine: with logits `[0, 1, 3, 2]` at `k = 2` the threshold is the second-LARGEST (2.0), so token 1 (logit 1.0) is correctly masked and the control had to observe token 3; a probe measured `k=2 -> {token 2, token 3}` and `k=1 -> {token 2}` before the assertion was fixed. Six mutants killed in total. Note for future rounds: running mutants over `sampling.rs` makes proptest regenerate `proptest-regressions/sampling/`, an artefact of testing mutated code rather than a real regression — deleted, not committed.
- **`renormalized_top_p_logprobs` is covered, and one "equivalent" mutant turned out to be a real overflow bug (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the only pre-existing test checked length, one `-inf`, and ordering, so 14 variants survived. Two new tests pin the actual values for the nucleus case (`top_p = 0.9` keeps two tokens renormalised to `e/(e+1)` and `1/(e+1)`, total mass 1) and the strict cutoff (`cumsum > top_p` at exact equality `[0, 0]` with `top_p = 0.5` must keep BOTH tokens; `>=` drops one). The most instructive finding was the `v - max_val` → `+` mutant. Arithmetically that is a constant shift, and softmax cancels constant shifts, so it *looks* equivalent — and with the obvious fixture (`max_val = 1.0`) it is, because the small-logit distributions are bit-identical. It is not equivalent: with `f32`, `exp(v + max_val)` overflows to `inf` once `max_val` passes roughly 88, the sum becomes `inf`, and every probability collapses to `NaN`, which then poisons the logprobs and the `SampleToken` JSON. That is precisely the failure the stabiliser exists to prevent. `renormalized_top_p_logprobs_stays_finite_for_large_logits` pins `[50.0, 49.0]` and kills it. The same investigation showed a related trap: the `v / max_val` variant is invisible at `max_val = 1.0` (dividing by one changes nothing), so the reference-value fixture was moved to `[2.0, 1.0, 0.0]` where the two distributions genuinely diverge. Two `>=` relaxations (`total > 0.0`, `p_norm > 0.0`) were confirmed equivalent with proofs and registered in `docs/mutation-equivalents.md`. Survivors for this function went 14 → 0 (2 excluded as equivalent).
- **`verify_missed_mutants.py` can no longer corrupt the tree or edit the wrong line (mutation tooling)** — this session lost real time to two failures of that script, both now fixed. (1) Its per-mutant `finally` only covers one iteration, so a run killed by timeout or an outer harness left the mutated source in place; the working tree then carried a fabricated `renormalized_top_p_logprobs` returning `vec![]`, and `cargo nextest` failed on code nobody had edited on purpose. It now tracks the live edit and restores it on every exit path, including SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers, and bounds each test command with `--timeout`. (2) A `--list --json` file goes stale the moment the source changes, and a stale span edits a DIFFERENT line than the one it names — a `-` → `/` mutation meant for `log_softmax` landed inside `renormalized_top_p_logprobs` and was then faithfully "restored", quietly breaking an unrelated function. The script now offers `--file`, which regenerates the mutant list from the current source instead of trusting a hand-carried JSON, and verifies each span's covered text still appears in the line the mutant's own diff claims to edit, aborting with `STALE SPAN` otherwise. Both directions were checked: a fresh `--file` run leaves the tree clean, and a deliberately shifted JSON is refused with the mismatched text quoted. Separately, the root cause of a confusing stretch of this round was an untracked background verification job still rewriting `sampling.rs` while I diagnosed "script bugs" — the two processes were fighting over the same file. The lesson recorded in the commit: a long-running mutation job must be tracked and stopped before any further verification.
- **`top_logprobs` ranking and the penalty guards are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `sample_one_with_params` had 21 genuine survivors, the largest cluster in `sampling.rs`. (1) `top_logprobs = Some(n) if n > 0` gates the whole ranking block; `cargo mutants` replaced that guard with `true` and `false`, flipped its `>` and inverted the `n_usize == 0` check, and every variant survived — because the only pre-existing `top_logprobs` test uses EMPTY logits, where the block yields `Vec::new()` either way. `top_logprobs_returns_the_ranked_most_likely_tokens` pins the populated path (exact ranking `[2, 1]`, descending order, agreement with the sampled token's own `logprob`, and `None` ⇒ empty) and kills 11 mutants on its own. (2) The penalty guards read `<penalty>… > EPSILON && !seen.is_empty()`. `penalties_apply_when_there_is_history` shows that deleting the `!` is a real inversion — a repeat penalty of 8.0 must demote the repeated argmax (logit 3.0 → 0.375, below token 2's 1.0) and the mutant keeps returning it. Two further variants are equivalent rather than gaps, established by measurement: `repeat_penalty - 1.0` → `+` passes the epsilon test for `penalty = 1.0` but then divides by 1.0, which is a no-op (probed: identical token and logprob), and the `>=` relaxation differs only at `|penalty - 1| == f32::EPSILON`, which no realistic params value produces.
- **`log_softmax`'s numerics and `top_p_sample`'s routing/distribution are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — the pre-existing `log_softmax` test asserted only `out[1] > out[2]`, which any monotone transformation satisfies, so all SIX arithmetic mutants survived: the `x - max_val` stabiliser, the `max_val + ln Σ` combination, and the final `x - log_sum_exp`. `log_softmax_matches_the_reference_values` pins the actual values (`-ln 2` for a uniform pair, `-ln(1 + e^-1)` and `-1 - ln(1 + e^-1)` for `[1, 0]`) plus the shift-invariance that the stabiliser exists to provide; all six mutants now fail. For `top_p_sample`, `top_p_sample_follows_the_renormalized_distribution` sweeps `random_threshold` across `[0, 1)` — making every draw deterministic so the empirical frequency IS the distribution — and checks the two-nucleus split against `e/(e+1)` and `1/(e+1)`; it kills five arithmetic mutants (the `x / sum` normalisation, the `cumsum += p` accumulation, the `p /= total` renormalisation and the `random_threshold <= cumsum` comparison). `top_p_at_or_above_one_always_returns_the_argmax` covers the `top_p >= 1.0 || logits.is_empty()` guard: switching `||` to `&&` sends a `top_p = 1.0` request down the nucleus path, so a caller asking for "no truncation" silently stops being greedy. Three survivors were investigated and left alone with reasons: `(v - max_val)` → `+` is mathematically equivalent (a constant factor cancels in the softmax — verified numerically), the `probs.len() - 1` fallback is unreachable once probabilities are normalised (`random_threshold < 1` always matches), and one reported case turned out to be an artefact of my own run: a `proptest-regressions/sampling/prop_tests.txt` was written while a mutant was applied, and the recorded case passes on committed code, so it was deleted rather than committed as a bogus regression seed.
- **`apply_repeat_penalty`'s index guards and `top_p_sample`'s strict nucleus cutoff are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — continuing the `sampling.rs` sweep (93 survivors left, down from 104). (1) The repeat-penalty loop guards `idx < logits.len()`; `cargo mutants` relaxed it to `<=` (an out-of-bounds index) and flipped it to `>`, and both survived because no fixture used a `seen` id at the final position or past the end. The new test penalises the LAST index, requires a past-the-end id to leave every logit untouched, and checks that a negative logit at the last index moves further from zero. (2) `top_p_sample` truncates the nucleus when `cumsum > top_p`; relaxing that to `>=` also cuts when the mass lands EXACTLY on `top_p`, dropping the token that completes the nucleus. With two equal logits the running mass is exactly 0.5, so `top_p = 0.5` separates the variants and the second token becomes unreachable under the mutant. Both new tests were verified to fail on their own mutant and pass otherwise. Two candidates were investigated and deliberately NOT papered over: `top_k_sample`'s `select_nth_unstable_by(top_k_limit - 1, …)` versus `top_k_limit` produces different thresholds (measured 19 vs 54 on `[0, 62, 27, 89, 54, 19]` at `k = 5`) yet no input makes the resulting SAMPLE differ, because softmax dominance keeps the low-ranked tokens unreachable at every threshold — so no test was added rather than one that cannot fail; and an early attempt at the `top_p` boundary assertion passed under the mutant because `str.replace(.., 1)` hit an identical expression in `renormalized_top_p_logprobs` one screen above, which is why the mutant must be applied by its recorded span rather than by text search.
- **`sampling.rs`'s RNG primitives are now covered, and three `memory.rs` equivalences are recorded (test coverage + mutation config, vllm-core)** — a first scan of `sampling.rs` (243 mutants) left 104 survivors, the largest single gap found so far, and the two most fundamental were real. (1) `random_f32` feeds every unseeded temperature/top-p draw; replacing its body with a constant `-1.0` — outside `[0, 1)` — passed the whole suite, because the existing `seed = None` test only checks that a token is still produced, which a constant also does. `random_f32_stays_in_the_unit_interval` draws 512 values and requires each to be in `[0, 1)` and not all identical. (2) `splitmix64` defines every seeded output; flipping `>>` to `<<` or `^` to `&` survived, so the reproducibility contract had no regression test. `splitmix64_matches_the_reference_finalizer` pins three vectors, cross-checked against an independently compiled copy of the published finalizer (an earlier hand-guessed vector was wrong and the test caught it). Each new test was verified to fail on its own mutant while the other still passes. Separately, `memory.rs`'s remaining survivors were triaged: `execute_preemption`'s `Prefilling` arm is unreachable in `running` (`requeue_partial_prefills` moves partial prefills back to the queue at the end of both update paths, and `build_batch` preempts before admitting new sequences), the queued branch of `cancel_request` inverts a guard around a no-op (a queued request owns no blocks — the running branch is a different matter and is covered), and the prefix-cache drain boundary needs an exact fit the caller never produces. All three are recorded in `docs/mutation-equivalents.md` and excluded. To stop the exclusion list from silently rotting, `scripts/check_mutant_exclusions.py` re-lists mutants with `--no-config` and fails if any pattern is STALE (line moved) or TOO WIDE (covers more than its documented rationale); verified both by passing on the current ten rules and by failing on a deliberately broken line number.
- **Mutation-scan noise is cut by recording equivalences, and the config now guards them (mutation testing)** — a fresh scan of `spec_dispatch/dispatch.rs` left 6 survivors, and every one turned out to be equivalent or already covered rather than an untested gap: (a) `trim_burst_at_stop`'s `stop_len > matched` — at equality `keep = matched - stop_len` is 0, so the whole burst is dropped and the flag has nothing to suppress (verified on a stop beginning at the burst's first token: the client stream is `[]` under both variants); (b) the outer `total_draft > 0` guard, which duplicates the callee's own `if total > 0`; (c) `ensure_verification_blocks`' `kv_block_ids.len() < blocks_needed`, since `ensure_blocks_for_tokens`' growth loop no-ops on equality; (d) `if elapsed > 0.0`, which differs only at exactly 0 where both skip. Rather than re-litigating these every night, `.cargo/mutants.toml` now excludes them with line-anchored patterns (so a refactor that moves the code makes the pattern miss loudly instead of silently widening the exclusion), and `docs/mutation-equivalents.md` records the evidence behind each, including the two rules for adding one: measure rather than reason, and never exclude a survivor you have not tried to kill. Net effect on the scans: `dispatch.rs` 56 → 52, `graph_step.rs` 18 → 17, `verify.rs` 49 → 48, `compose/prefill.rs` 24 → 23. The document also preserves one case that is unobservable rather than equivalent — `cleanup_priority_queue` cannot be distinguished from a no-op through the current interface (`dequeue` already skips stale entries and the heap length is not exposed), so it stays in the scan as a known limitation instead of being hidden.
- **Speculative step latency and `RequestQueue`'s lookup/identity are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — a fresh scan of `spec_dispatch/dispatch.rs` (56 mutants) and a first scan of `request_queue.rs` (27 mutants). (1) `step_speculative_inner`'s `if elapsed > 0.0 { record_latency(elapsed) }` looked equivalent for the same reason the graph path's copy did: a fast mock finishes under a millisecond, so the guard never fires. It is a fixture property, not a code property — with a 5 ms backend the guard fires and the `<` / `==` mutants drop every sample, leaving `avg_latency_ms` at 0 (measured 25 vs 0) while the engine still serves. Speculative decode is exactly the path whose tail latency an operator watches, so the samples must exist; `speculative_step_records_its_latency` pins `avg_latency_ms >= 1.0` and both mutants now fail. (2) `RequestQueue::get_mut` had no test at all — replacing its body with `None` passed the suite, so a caller updating a queued sequence in place would silently stop working; the new test mutates through the reference and reads it back, and also requires `None` for an unknown id. (3) `ScheduledSequence`'s `PartialEq` compares only `seq_id`, but the type is only ever stored in a `BinaryHeap`, which uses `Ord` — so its `true` / `false` / inverted variants all survived. The new test pins id-based identity (same id with different priority and arrival time must compare equal) so `Eq` and `Ord` keep agreeing on what makes two entries the same entity. Recorded rather than chased: `cleanup_priority_queue` is a memory-reclamation pass whose mutants are unobservable — `dequeue` already skips stale entries in its `while let`, and the heap's internal length is not exposed, so no fixture can distinguish it from a no-op.
- **SJF's weighted score and both non-default policy names are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — scanning `scheduler/policy` (14 mutants) left 5 survivors, all real. (1) `SjfPolicy::compute_priority` scores `pw * user_priority + rw * remaining_tokens`; replacing that `*` with `+` survived because the existing fixture's default weights order the two sequences identically under both formulas. The new test uses `SjfPolicy::new(10.0, 0.5)` with a long low-priority sequence and a short high-priority one, where the product gives 50 vs 91 (long wins) and the sum gives 100.5 vs 92.5 (short wins) — the orderings genuinely disagree, so the mutant now fails. (2) `PriorityPolicy::name` and `SjfPolicy::name` replaced with `""` or `"xyzzy"` both survived: only `FcfsPolicy` had a name assertion, yet the name is what logs and metrics label a scheduling decision with, so a wrong string silently mis-attributes telemetry. `every_policy_reports_a_distinct_name` pins all three and requires them non-empty and pairwise distinct. Also added `scripts/verify_missed_mutants.py`, which re-applies a scan's recorded span edits by hand and re-runs a test command, because cargo-mutants' MISSED verdicts have repeatedly been wrong in this tree; its docstring records the limits — the recorded span is the author's best guess rather than a guarantee, and a survivor still needs a human call between real gap, inexpressible fixture, and equivalence. That script is what exposed a mis-attribution: `prefill.rs:168:37` is `tokens_to_process > chunk_cap` (not the `> budget_room` guard one line above); its `>=` mutant is equivalent because `min(cap, cap) == cap`, and the new boundary test documents that rather than pretending to kill it. `lifecycle.rs` scanned clean (22 of 24 caught, 1 unviable, 1 tool timeout).
- **The CUDA-graph step-latency metric is covered, correcting an earlier "equivalent" call (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `record_batch_metrics`' guard `if elapsed > 0.0` was previously judged equivalent because `elapsed` is `as_millis()` and a fast mock step finishes under a millisecond, so the guard never fired. That is a property of the FIXTURE, not of the code: with a 5 ms mock the guard does fire, and the `<` / `==` mutants drop every sample, leaving `avg_latency_ms` at 0 while the engine still serves normally — latency is the metric an operator relies on to see a slow graph step at all. `cuda_graph_step_records_latency_for_a_slow_step` pins it with a deliberately slow backend and requires `avg_latency_ms >= 1.0`; the `<` and `==` variants now fail. Only `>` -> `>=` stays equivalent, and genuinely so: the two differ solely at `elapsed == 0`, where both skip. Also characterised during the `batch_composer` re-scan (survivors 25 -> 14): `prefill.rs:189`'s four mutants sit on a `tracing::debug!` conditional and its `remaining` field, so they are behaviour-neutral; the two `builder()` methods only wrap `Builder::default()`, which is what `Default::default()` already produces; and the packing dispatch guard's variants are all equivalent because `compose_prefill_with_packing` currently just delegates to `compose_prefill_batch` (verified by flipping the guard three ways with zero test failures). Two cases the scanner still reports as missed (`prefill.rs:168` `> budget_room`, `chunked.rs:64` slice arithmetic) fail their tests when applied by hand — a cargo-mutants attribution artefact, not a coverage gap.
- **The CUDA-graph metrics and the non-graph `step_with_graph` fallback are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — re-scanning `graph_step.rs` with `--features cuda-graph` after the stale-mask fixture dropped survivors from 11 to 6, and the two remaining behaviour gaps were real. (1) `record_batch_metrics` books the token count, phase split, batch size and latency; `cargo mutants` replaced the WHOLE function with `()` and every test passed, so an engine running graphs would have reported zero throughput and zero batch size to `/metrics` while serving normally. `cuda_graph_step_records_batch_metrics` pins `tokens_total` (4 for `max_tokens = 4`) and `avg_batch_size` (1.0), both independent of wall-clock. (2) `Engine::step_with_graph` has TWO definitions: the `#[cfg(feature = "cuda-graph")]` one and a fallback that warns and delegates to `step()`. The only tests calling `step_with_graph` are both gated behind that feature, so the plain build's copy was never executed — `Ok(vec![])` there survived. `fallback_step_with_graph_runs_the_regular_step` runs on default features and pins both the per-step result length and the full 4-token stream. `record_batch_metrics`' `if elapsed > 0.0` guard remains equivalent: `elapsed` is `as_millis()` and measured 0 on every graph step in this environment, so `>`, `>=`, `==` and `<` skip exactly the same samples.
- **The CUDA-graph step path's mid-chunk suppression is covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `Engine::process_output` (graph path) computes its own `stale_mask` with `batch.num_computed_tokens[i] + batch.input_tokens[i].len() < s.prompt_len`, a duplicate of the predicate the CPU path applies. `cargo mutants` (with `--features cuda-graph`) flipped that `<` to `>` and the whole suite stayed green: `chunked_prefill_never_streams_stale_midchunk_predictions` drives `engine.step()`, never `step_with_graph()`. Inverted, the mask marks the WRONG rounds — stale mid-chunk predictions reach the client and the real final-chunk token is withheld. `cuda_graph_step_never_streams_stale_midchunk_predictions` mirrors the CPU scenario through the graph path (capture, then `step_with_graph`) and pins the exact stream `[111, 222 x 7]`; verified to fail on the inverted predicate while the CPU twin still passes, proving the mutant really was graph-only. That one fixture also kills the graph path's `step_with_graph -> Ok(vec![])` mutant. Characterised rather than chased: `record_batch_metrics`' `if elapsed > 0.0` latency guard is equivalent in practice — `elapsed` is `as_millis()`, measured as 0 on every graph step here, so `>` / `>=` / `<` all skip the same samples; and `execute_regular -> Ok(Default::default())` is unviable (`BatchOutput` has no `Default`), not untested.
- **The resolver draft route's position arithmetic is covered, closing the `generate_per_seq_drafts` gap (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `step_speculative_inner` has two draft routes: `generate_batched_drafts` (only `draft_model` set) and `generate_per_seq_drafts` (a `draft_resolver` is present). They duplicate the position math (`base = num_computed_tokens + input_len - 1`, then `base + pos` for `pos in 0..=max_draft`), and every existing test drove the BATCHED route — `with_draft_resolver` is crate-private, so no in-crate test had ever built a resolver with a usable backend. Two mutants had survived for that reason. `resolver_draft_path_generates_at_consecutive_positions` builds a resolver whose self-spec backend records the positions it is called with, then asserts both the positions (`[2, 3, 4]` for a 3-token prompt) and the DRAFT COUNT. The count assertion is what distinguishes the `pos >= max_draft` guard: the loop runs `max_draft + 1` times so the final iteration writes the last slot's KV and discards its prediction, and the mutant (keeping it) hands the verifier 6 tokens instead of 5 — a silent one-draft over-budget that no position-only assertion can see. Verified both ways: the position mutant yields `[0, 1, 2]` and the guard mutant yields `forward_logits` length 6.
- **The chunked-prefill budget guard, its token accumulator, and `with_packing`'s constructor are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — three more `batch_composer` survivors from the mutation scan, each a genuine gap rather than an equivalent. (1) The chunk-admission guard `total_tokens + tokens_to_process > max_token_budget` was never driven to an EXACT fit; `>` -> `>=` survived, and the variant drops a chunk that fits perfectly (the sequence then waits another round for no reason). Two tests pin exact fit (admitted) and one-token-short (rejected). (2) `total_tokens += tokens_to_process` was unguarded: `+=` -> `*=` pins the accumulator at 0 (0 times anything is 0), so the budget is never seen as consumed and EVERY sequence is admitted regardless of overrun. A single-sequence fixture cannot show it — the accumulator is never read again — so the test uses two, and the mutant admits `[1, 2]` where only `[1]` fits. (3) `BatchComposer::with_packing` replaced by `Self::default()` survived the suite: the default budget is 4096 and every existing test stays far under it, so a dropped config looked identical to a kept one. The test uses `max_batch_size = 1`, where the fallback to 256 admits both sequences. Also recorded: `compose_with_chunking` (and therefore `compose_chunked_prefill`) has no production caller — it is a test-only helper — so mutants there are reachable only through tests, which is why they are worth pinning but do not represent a live-path risk.
- **`batch_composer`'s chunk-size tiers, auto mode, and prefill classification are now covered (test coverage, vllm-core)** — scanning the module (76 mutants) left 25 survivors, two of which were real strategy gaps rather than equivalents. (1) `ChunkedPrefillConfig::calculate_chunk_size` caps the chunk at 512 above 8192 tokens and 1024 above 4096, but the only existing test used `seq_len` 32 and 1000, so BOTH tier guards were unvisited — `>` -> `>=`, `==` and `<` on those lines all survived, i.e. the caps that keep one prefill chunk from OOMing a long prompt were never verified. `test_chunk_size_tiers_respect_their_boundaries` pins 4096/4097/8192/8193 (kills all five tier mutants). (2) Auto mode (`target_chunk_size == 0`) had NO test at all: every existing case set a non-zero target, so replacing its `/` with `*` survived even though the two differ by three orders of magnitude (a 100 KiB budget at 128 bytes/token is an 800-token chunk; multiplying gives 13 million, which the clamp then pins to `max_chunk_size` for every input). `test_auto_chunk_size_derives_from_the_memory_budget` covers the divide, the `min_chunk_size` floor and the `max_chunk_size` ceiling. (3) `classify_prefill_seq`'s chunking decisions gained an inline test module covering a fresh prompt longer than the chunk cap (not split), a resumed prefill longer than one chunk (split), a remainder under the cap (not re-split), an exact budget fit (runs whole — this one distinguishes the `> budget_room` guard, which is equivalent at smaller room but diverges once the room exceeds the chunk cap), an exhausted budget (breaks) and a fully-computed sequence (skipped). Recorded rather than chased: `compose_prefill_with_packing` currently just delegates to `compose_prefill_batch`, so the packing dispatch guard's `true`/`false` mutants are true equivalents.
- **Duplicated dead `argmax` removed, `generate_per_seq_drafts`' position math pinned, and phase-2 preemption characterised (dead code + test coverage, vllm-core)** — three findings from scanning previously-unscanned modules. (1) `spec_dispatch/drafts.rs` carried a public `argmax` marked `#[allow(dead_code)]` whose own doc said it duplicated `vllm_traits::argmax_logits`; it had zero callers (the verifier delegates to `sample_one_with_params`), and four of its mutants survived — the tool was grading a function nobody could call. Removed. It is not in the public-API surface (the module is private), so the API check correctly reported no change. (2) `generate_per_seq_drafts`/`generate_batched_drafts` compute `base = num_computed_tokens + input_len - 1` and decode at `base + pos`; `+` -> `*` on the position survives the suite because every existing test uses a 1- or 0-based fixture where the variants coincide. `drafts_are_generated_at_consecutive_positions_after_the_prompt` records the actual positions the draft backend is called with (two steps, so the second one has a non-zero frontier) and pins the consecutive sequence — it kills the position mutant (verified), while the `base` mutant needs the `draft_resolver` path (`generate_per_seq_drafts` is only reached when `draft_resolver.is_some()`), which is left as the next concrete lead. (3) `execute_preemption`'s phase-2 accounting (`blocks_freed += block_count`) survives, and instrumenting it showed WHY: phase 2's `preemptable` list is ALWAYS empty in every configuration probed, because phase 1 moves every sequence owning a victim block out of `running` first. The `+=` mutant is therefore unreachable from the production path, not untested.
- **`SchedulerEngine::memory_rollback` removed as dead code, and `ensure_blocks_for_tokens`' exact-fit behaviour is now covered (dead code + test coverage, vllm-core)** — `memory_rollback` had ZERO callers anywhere in the workspace, only self-referential mentions in comments and its own doc-comment claiming it was "kept as public API for tests and non-speculative callers" — neither of which existed. It was the engine-level wrapper around the rollback path the speculative step deliberately stopped using (rejected-draft KV is unused capacity beyond `num_computed_tokens`; rewinding the frontier against the PRE-step count released blocks holding real prefix KV on a resumed prefill). Its doc-comment already said the speculative step must not call it. Removed, with the module doc corrected to explain why no rollback entry point exists. `MemoryManager::rollback` itself stays — it has real unit tests pinning its refcount release and is a genuine library capability. Separately, the growth loop in `ensure_blocks_for_tokens` is `while kv_blocks.len() < min_blocks`; `cargo mutants` relaxed it to `<=` and the suite still passed, because the sequence then gets ONE block more than asked on every call (unused capacity, so no correctness signal, but a real KV leak on every speculative step). `test_ensure_blocks_for_tokens_allocates_only_the_shortfall` pins both the exact-fit no-op and the one-block growth. Also classified: the remaining `memory.rs` survivors are `#[cfg(feature = "multi-node")]`-only (verified by re-scanning with `--features multi-node`, which cut survivors 19 → 10), and `execute_preemption`'s `available_blocks() < blocks_needed` boundary is structurally near-equivalent — callers only invoke it when `additional > available`, and `blocks_needed` is the sequence's WHOLE table, so exact equality is not reachable from the production path. Public API: -1 `SchedulerEngine::memory_rollback`.
- **`cancel_request`'s KV release is covered, and the mutation timeout is now a measured value instead of a guess (test coverage + CI, vllm-core)** — `cargo mutants` deleted the `!` from `if !seq.kv_blocks.is_empty()` in `cancel_request` (both branches) and the whole suite still passed: every existing cancel test asserts only the `bool` return value and response-channel cleanup, never the allocator effect. `test_cancel_request_releases_kv_blocks` batches a request so it holds real blocks, cancels it, and requires `used == 0`; the running-branch mutant now fails with `4 still used`. The queued-branch mutant is equivalent (a queued request owns no blocks, so `is_empty()` is always true) and is recorded as such rather than chased. Separately, `--timeout` is cargo-mutants' bound on ONE mutant's whole build+test cycle, not a single test: the core suite runs in ~8s but 8-way build contention pushes a cycle past 30s, and the tool then reports TIMEOUT — which the score check counts as UNDETECTED. Measured directly: a 30s cap produced 12 bogus timeouts on `engine/spec_dispatch/verify.rs`, and 60s cleared every one (the 2 survivors left are genuine, one of which — `bonus_offset` `+` -> `-` — fails 21 tests when run directly, so cargo-mutants' own harness misses it for unrelated reasons). Both the nightly workflow and `just mutants` now use 60s.
- **Two unverified verifier behaviours are now covered: the `max_tokens` budget-cap position and the decode-round counter (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `cargo mutants` on `engine/spec_dispatch/verify.rs` and `scheduler/engine/update.rs` found two survivors that were genuinely unguarded. (1) The budget-cap branch samples its extra token from `(input_len - 1 + accepted) * vocab_size`; replacing that `+` with `*` survives the suite and is observably wrong — a 2-token prefill with `max_tokens = 3` against a `+1` model streams `[7, 8, 6]` instead of `[7, 8, 9]`, because the token is sampled from the wrong position. The existing budget tests assert only the token COUNT, which the mutant preserves. `budget_cap_samples_from_the_position_after_the_accepted_drafts` pins the actual token IDs. (2) `consecutive_decode_rounds += 1` drives eviction priority (it flips `compute_priority` at > 5) and is part of the victim-set cache key, but every existing test sets it by hand on a hand-built `Sequence`; replacing `+= 1` with `*= 1` (pinning it at 0, so a sequence is never considered long-running) survived. `test_push_token_advances_consecutive_decode_rounds` pins the production increment. Also characterised, rather than papered over: the verifier's `offset + vocab_size > logits.len()` guard cannot trigger (measured `end` maxes out at `logits_len - vocab`), so its `>` -> `>=` mutant is equivalent, and cargo-mutants' 12 "timeouts" on this file were an artefact of its 30s cap — a 300s re-scan resolved them all, and one remaining "miss" (`bonus_offset` `+` -> `-`) fails 20 tests when run directly.
- **Preemption's requeue invariant is covered on CPU, replacing two CUDA-gated assertions that could never fail (test coverage, vllm-core)** — `gpu_scheduler_preempts_under_memory_pressure` asserted `waiting > 0 || !running.is_empty()` and `gpu_engine_e2e_graceful_shutdown` asserted `!has_pending() || !running.is_empty()`. Both are tautologies: `Engine::has_pending` IS `!queue.is_empty() || !running.is_empty()`, so the second reduces to `(empty && nothing running) || running` and the first is true in every state after any admission. Both also sit behind `#[ignore = "requires CUDA GPU hardware"]`, so they never ran in CI at all. `test_scheduler_memory_pressure_requeues_preempted_sequence` drives the same situation on CPU with a block budget of one, and asserts what the originals were reaching for: a sequence that WAS `Decoding` (verified absent from the waiting queue first, so a later requeue cannot mask a broken preemption path) is moved into the waiting queue with its KV blocks released and its frontier reset. Verified by mutation: dropping the phase-1 requeue fails it (`running=0, waiting=1`), keeping the blocks on preemption fails it. New `SchedulerEngine::request_queue()` read-only accessor (+1 public method) since `get_sequence` only searches `running`. The CUDA-gated tautologies themselves were left untouched (they cannot be executed here, so rewriting them unverified would be worse than flagging them).
- **`is_healthy`'s error threshold is now covered, via a `FailingModel` fixture (test coverage, vllm-core + vllm-testing)** — `cargo mutants` on `engine/lifecycle.rs` replaced `Engine::is_healthy` with a constant `true` and the entire workspace still passed: every existing assertion checks only the healthy direction (`assert!(engine.is_healthy())` in `e2e_error_recovery.rs` and `integration.rs`), so the documented "10 or more errors ⇒ unhealthy" threshold had no coverage at all. No fixture could drive it — every model in `vllm-testing` succeeds — so a new `FailingModel` (all three forward entry points return `ModelError`) was added. `test_is_healthy_turns_false_after_error_threshold` pins both sides of the boundary (9 healthy, 10 not) and then drives a real `Engine::run` loop over the failing backend to prove the counter rises through the production path. Kills both the constant-`true` and the `<` → `<=` mutants. Note: a direct `step()` error does NOT bump `error_count` — only the `run` loop's error arm does, which is why the test spawns the loop. Public API: +1 `vllm_testing::FailingModel`.
- **The nightly mutation job now actually scans what its baselines claim, and a dead branch it exposed is gone (CI + correctness, vllm-core)** — three separate faults meant the job gated nothing. (1) `cargo mutants --file` takes a GLOB, not a directory, so the `scheduler/policy` entry (and the justfile's directory branch) matched ZERO files: verified `--file crates/core/src/scheduler/policy` lists 0 mutants while `--file 'crates/core/src/scheduler/policy/**/*.rs'` lists 14, meaning that module's 95% baseline had never been checked. (2) `scripts/check_mutation_score.sh` read an `outcomes.json` that cargo-mutants 27.x does not produce (it writes `caught.txt` / `missed.txt` / `timeout.txt` / `unviable.txt`), so every real scan exited 2; it now reads those files, counts `timeout` as undetected (it previously vanished from both sides of the ratio) while leaving `unviable` out, and prints the undetected mutants on failure. (3) the render step selected `.status == "SURVIVED"` from `mutants.json`, a field that does not exist, so the survived-mutant summary was always empty. `engine/spec_dispatch` is now in the scan matrix (127 mutants, observed 83.67% → 75% floor) — the module that produced this session's mid-burst stop and mid-burst EOS defects had never been scanned. The scan immediately surfaced a real one: flipping `completes_stop || ended_on_stop` to `&&` in `emit_verified_tokens` leaked every tail stop, and no test noticed. Instrumenting it showed `completes_stop` had become UNREACHABLE — the fold now trims a stop-completed burst before the send loop, so `check_completion` has already run and only `ended_on_stop` can fire — so the dead half was removed rather than propped up with a test, and `speculative_tail_stop_with_budget_left_is_withheld` pins the behaviour that still matters.
- **The speculative prefill step's frontier advance now has a test that fails when it is wrong, and a permanently-inert cap on it was removed (test + clarity, vllm-core)** — last round's mutation testing found that no test in the workspace detected dropping `accepted` from `chunk_len + accepted` in `fold_speculative_update`, because decode batches take their frontier from `seq.tokens.len() - 1` (`compose/decode.rs`) and only a sequence still `Prefilling` reads `seq.num_computed_tokens`. The new `test_speculative_prefill_step_advances_frontier_over_accepted_drafts` pins the invariant decode depends on — a `Decoding` sequence's recorded frontier equals `tokens.len() - 1` — and fails on that mutation (`num_computed_tokens=2` where 6 is required). Instrumenting the fold also showed the `min(kept - 1)` cap added last round never binds (`accepted == kept - 1` in every observed step): when a stop cuts the burst the sequence is finalized immediately, so the frontier is never read again. It was removed along with its incorrect justification — `input_count` is how many positions the target model computed KV for (the `input_len + drafts` verification span), not how many tokens were kept for the client.
- **Three tautological test assertions replaced with ones that can actually fail (test hygiene, vllm-core)** — all three were written as disjunctions that are true in nearly every reachable state, so the tests passed regardless of the behaviour they claimed to guard. `test_speculative_kv_reuse_accepted_prefix` asserted `engine.has_pending() || !engine.has_pending()` (true unconditionally — literally a no-op); `test_speculative_kv_append_draft_tokens_to_input` asserted `has_pending() || running_count() > 0 || !outputs.is_empty()`; `test_scheduler_concurrent_requests` asserted `running_count() > 0 || waiting_count() > 0 || !has_pending()` under the comment "should have processed some requests". The first two are now pinned to the emitted/recorded-token accounting of the sequence (which the `IncrementModel` fixture genuinely exercises — a mutation that stops folding tokens into `seq.tokens` makes the engine loop forever, and dropping the frontier advance is correctly NOT detected by these fixture tests, which is why they no longer claim to cover it); the third asserts all 10 admitted requests stay tracked in `running + waiting` (verified by mutation: dropping one admission fails with `running=9`).
- **15 speculative tests are back in the default gate — their `#[ignore = "requires speculative model setup"]` reason had gone stale (test hygiene, vllm-core)** — the marker was originally accurate: the assertions needed a real draft/target checkpoint pair. The fixtures have since moved to `IncrementModel` / `ConstModel` mocks and the code paths they cover (`SelfSpeculativeModel`, multi-draft verification, KV block tables during verification, bounded KV overhead), so every one of them passes without a checkpoint — but they stayed `#[ignore]`d and never ran in `just nextest` / `just nextest-default`, i.e. 15 real assertions about the speculative path were only exercised by the slow `--run-ignored` sweep nobody runs by default. Verified stable across three consecutive runs before stripping the markers from `adaptive_speculative.rs` (3), `speculative_kv_cache.rs` (7), `speculative_memory_overhead.rs` (4) and `integration.rs` (1). `test_speculative_kv_append_draft_tokens_to_input` also lost a tautological disjunction assertion (`has_pending() || running_count() > 0 || !outputs.is_empty()`, true in almost any state) for a real check that the decode step produced output for the running sequence. Default-feature gate: 2382 → 2398 tests, 53 → 38 skips; core now runs with 0 skips. The remaining `#[ignore]`s are the genuine CUDA-hardware and on-disk-checkpoint gates.
- **Two concurrent speculative requests no longer stream their output twice or overshoot `max_tokens` (correctness, vllm-core)** — `fold_speculative_update` folded one sequence per `update_speculative` call, and that call always ends with `finalize_finished_sequences` + `requeue_partial_prefills`, which walk the WHOLE running set. A freshly admitted prefill is admitted at `num_computed_tokens = 0`, so while the first sequence was being folded the still-unfolded second one satisfied `num_computed_tokens < prompt_len` and was requeued as a "mid-chunk prefill" — its just-emitted tokens dropped from `seq.tokens` (they had already been sent to the client). The next round regenerated and re-streamed them, so a 2-request batch produced e.g. `[7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12]` — 11 tokens for `max_tokens = 6` — for the second sequence. The fold now collects every sequence's tokens and issues ONE `update_speculative` call, so the cleanup/requeue passes run once at the end, after all sequences are folded. 1 new regression test (2 identical greedy requests must each stream exactly `max_tokens` tokens once).
- **A `stop` that completes inside a speculative burst — and an EOS token generated mid-burst — now end the sequence at the match point instead of streaming through (correctness, vllm-core)** — the speculative path folds a whole burst of accepted drafts into `seq.tokens` in one update, so the terminator was no longer at the tail when the send loop and `finalize_stop_sequences` looked at it: both are tail/suffix checks. `stop: [[8, 9]]` against a stream of 7, 8, 9, 10, … delivered the stop AND every token after it and kept decoding to `max_tokens`; with an EOS id configured the same burst shape streamed the EOS and then a full `max_tokens` of tokens the model never meant to emit. `fold_speculative_update` now cuts each non-stale burst at the first terminator, advances the computed frontier by the kept tokens only, emits the kept prefix and finalizes with `FinishReason::Stop`; a multi-token `stop` that began in an earlier burst releases nothing (its opening tokens are in the send loop's hold-back buffer and are discarded, not flushed as output). EOS keeps its token — matching `step_regular`, where the HTTP layer drops the control token's text. 2 new regression tests (`speculative_mid_burst_stop_is_withheld` un-ignored + assertions on the stream, the end of the sequence and the finish reason; `speculative_stop_spanning_two_bursts_is_withheld`) + `speculative_mid_burst_eos_ends_the_sequence`.
- **`--config` now takes precedence over `$VLLM_CONFIG_PATH` (config-honesty, vllm-server)** — the env var silently overrode an explicit `--config` flag, so `vllm-server --config /tmp/test.yaml` inside a deployment exporting `VLLM_CONFIG_PATH` ran the deployment config with zero signal, contradicting the documented precedence (`CLI flags > env > YAML`, OPERATIONS.md:125). The explicit flag now wins; both sources set logs a `WARN` naming each; env-only deployments are unaffected (env path still used). (RIL ISS-169.)
- **`/v1/completions` now rejects a `stop` set whose strings all tokenize to zero tokens with `400 invalid_request_error`, matching chat (API contract parity, vllm-server)** — the chat path refused the same degenerate input while completions silently warned and skipped stop-sequence matching, so a client that set `stop: ["…"]` against completions could receive output it explicitly asked to halt with a `200`. `tokenize_stop_sequences` now errors identically to `tokenize_chat_stop_sequences`, threaded through all three completions spawn paths (single-shot, `best_of`, `n > 1`). (RIL ISS-164.)
- **Streaming `chunk.id` is now a unique per-request `chatcmpl-<uuid>` / `cmpl-<uuid>` instead of the shared `"chatcmpl-stream"` / `"cmpl-stream"` constant (API contract, vllm-server)** — OpenAI's SSE contract treats the chunk `id` as the identifier of ONE completion; every streamed chat/completions response in the process previously emitted the identical constant, so a client deduping or correlating streams by `id` collided across concurrent requests. Each stream now stamps one uuid once (n > 1 streams merge candidates into a single id; n = 1 streams carry the same id on every chunk, matching the non-streaming convention). (RIL ISS-175.)
- **`CudaGraph::execute` refuses to replay a graph that captured zero nodes instead of silently returning `Ok(())` (correctness, vllm-model)** — `capture()` is a CPU-side stub that only sets `cached`, so every graph `BatchCudaGraphExecutor::capture_all_graphs` builds is empty; replaying it previously succeeded as a no-op and the executor then fabricated `TokenId::default()` (0) output for every sequence, which `Engine::step_with_graph` would feed to `process_output` as soon as anyone wires the capture call. An empty capture now surfaces `CudaGraphError::CaptureFailed`, mapping to `GraphExecutionFailed` so the engine falls back to the eager forward instead of emitting token-0 garbage. (RIL ISS-176.)
- public-api: vllm-server added 4 `ConfigValidationError` variants — `MaxBatchSizeTooLarge`, `MaxWaitingBatchesOutOfRange`, `MaxModelLenTooLarge`, `TensorParallelSizeTooLarge` — mirroring the CLI flag bounds on the YAML config path (RIL ISS-154).
- public-api: vllm-server added `ConfigValidationError::MaxModelLenZero` — the YAML `engine.max_model_len` lower bound (must be >= 1, mirroring the CLI `--max-model-len` range); `0` previously passed validation and rejected every request (RIL ISS-167).
- **`/v1/embeddings` rejects an unsupported `encoding_format` instead of silently returning un-encoded floats (API contract, vllm-server)** — OpenAI's embeddings contract accepts `encoding_format: "float" | "base64"`; the endpoint previously ignored the field and returned plain floats with 200, so a base64 client decoded garbage.
