The field is now declared on `EmbeddingsRequest` (default/omitted = `float`) and the handler 400s any non-`"float"` value, naming the offending format. (RIL ISS-161.)
- public-api: vllm-server added `EmbeddingsRequest::encoding_format` (`Option<String>`, default `None` = `"float"` — declared so the handler can explicitly reject the unimplemented `"base64"` form instead of silently dropping it) (RIL ISS-161).
- **`/v1/completions` gains request lifecycle logs and chat's request logs carry `model` (observability, vllm-server)** — the legacy completions endpoint had ZERO request-lifetime tracing (only a stop-sequence warning), so a completions quality/regression was untraceable without re-running; chat's non-stream "Request started"/"Request completed" lines omitted `model`, the single most important attribute for "which model served this request". Completions now logs a start line (`request_id`, `model`, `stream`, `prompt_tokens`, `max_tokens`) after validation and completion lines on the best_of and single-shot paths; chat's non-stream lines gain `model=`. Field set matches the streaming chat convention so all lifecycle lines have the same shape. (RIL ISS-155.)
- **`vllm` CLI subcommands render help descriptions (usability, vllm-server bin)** — `vllm --help` showed `config`/`model` with blank descriptions and the leaf commands (validate/list/info) too, so a fresh operator learned nothing about each command from help. clap derives the per-subcommand `about` from variant doc-comments, which were missing; they now carry descriptions at every level, guarded by a test that walks the command tree. (RIL ISS-153.)
- **Inference endpoints now `404 model_not_found` for a requested model that isn't loaded (API contract, vllm-server)** — chat, completions, and embeddings accepted ANY non-empty `model` id and served it with the loaded model, echoing the wrong id back with `200`; a typo'd `"gpt-4o"` against a Qwen deployment silently produced output from the wrong model, and monitoring/proxy stacks keying routing on `response.model` mis-attributed generations. The new `validate_model_conformance` helper rejects a non-matching id with OpenAI's `404 model_not_found` (`The model 'X' does not exist or you do not have access to it`, `invalid_request_error`). The check is **strict only when the server can name its model** — a fallback/stub tokenizer (with no name) stays lenient, preserving the `Tokenizer::new()` test seam. Wired into chat (including the streaming path), completions (`model: None` stays the legacy server-default), and embeddings. Reverses the undocumented completions "echo verbatim" proxy convention (DEC-059). (RIL ISS-152.)
- **`/health/details` no longer fabricates `0.0` throughput / KV-usage when the engine is mid-step (observability truthfulness, vllm-server)** — on a `GetMetrics` timeout (a long decode on a large batch), the handler previously wrapped the default snapshot in `Some`, emitting `"prefill_throughput": 0.0` / `"kv_cache_usage_percent": 0.0` — indistinguishable from an idle or dead engine to dashboards. It now keeps the `Option`: `None` on timeout / closed channel, and both fields are omitted from the wire (`skip_serializing_if = is_none`, the ISS-141 no-null-lie rule), so a dashboard reading `null` knows the engine simply didn't answer. A new test drives an engine worker that answers `GetMetrics` and asserts the fields ARE present with real values. (RIL ISS-151.)
- **Config-validation failures now print every violation to stderr before exiting (usability, vllm-server)** — `app_config.validate()` errors were emitted via `tracing::error!`, but that runs before `logging::init_logging` installs a subscriber, so an invalid `--config` (port 0, `num_kv_blocks` too large, `max_draft_tokens` > 64) exited 78 with **no message at all** — a supervisor sees just the config-error code, the operator sees nothing. Violations now print to stderr (`invalid: ...`, matching `vllm config validate`) before `exit(78)`. (RIL ISS-148.)
- **`--log-dir` with an uncreatable directory now fails fast instead of panicking (usability, vllm-server)** — `init_logging` used `create_dir_all(&dir).ok()`, swallowing the error so a typo'd `--log-dir` (parent is a file, or no write permission) panicked deep inside `tracing-appender`'s `RollingFileAppender::new` — a raw init stack trace and exit 101. It now prints `log dir error: <reason>` to stderr and exits 1. (RIL ISS-150.)
- **Model-load errors name the exact config file (usability, vllm-model)** — `ModelLoader`'s two `config.json` read paths reported `Failed to read config: No such file...` with no path, so a typo'd / missing `--model` dir (the most common startup mistake) surfaced as an un-actionable "some config" error. Both now name the file (`Failed to read /tmp/no-such-model/config.json: ...`). (RIL ISS-149.)
- **`Tokenizer::from_file` reports the real model id from the `--model` directory instead of a hardcoded `Qwen3.5-0.8B` (obs-honesty, vllm-model)** — every real checkpoint loaded via `tokenizer.json` advertised the fabricated identity `Qwen3.5-0.8B` to `/v1/models`, `/v1/models/{id}`, and `/health/details`, so a Llama/Mistral deploy looked like a Qwen to dashboards, canary checks, and OpenAI SDK clients that pin the model id. The id is now derived from the parent directory of `tokenizer.json` (= the `--model` dir the operator pointed at); `None` for a bare filename. Unit test covers the derivation helper directly (no `tokenizer.json` on disk). (RIL ISS-147.)
- **`BatchManager::get_all_jobs` returns newest-first deterministically (correctness, vllm-server)** — `BatchListResponse.data`'s doc claims "newest first", but the manager returned `HashMap` iteration order — arbitrary, and varying across calls as the map is rebuilt, so a client taking `data[0]` as "latest batch" got an arbitrary one. A monotonic `created_seq` (manager-owned `AtomicU64`, assigned at `create_job`) breaks the same-second `created_at` tie; iteration is now `(created_at desc, created_seq desc)`. `created_seq` is `pub(crate)` (wire types don't expose internal ordering). (RIL ISS-146.)
- **Embeddings accept a single-string `input` like OpenAI (API contract, vllm-server)** — OpenAI's `input` is `string | array<string>`, but `EmbeddingsRequest.input: Vec<String>` rejected the single-string form (`"input": "the quick brown fox"` — one of the most common embeddings calls) at the serde boundary with `400 expected a sequence`. A `deserialize_with` boundary helper now normalizes `string` → one-element list while keeping the Rust field `Vec<String>`, so the array path is unchanged. (RIL ISS-144.)
- **Wrong-verb requests answer `405` with the OpenAI error envelope instead of axum's plain-text `Method Not Allowed` (usability, vllm-server)** — `POST /v1/models`, `GET /v1/chat/completions`, etc. fell through to axum's default `405` handling whose `text/plain` body breaks the `{error:{...}}` parsing every OpenAI SDK performs on non-2xx — the exact class of SDK-breaking the ISS-125 404 fallback fixed. `build_app` now registers `.method_not_allowed_fallback` returning `405` + `{"error":{"message":"Method Not Allowed","type":"invalid_request_error"}}`. (RIL ISS-145.)
- **`vllm model info` now errors on a directory without `config.json` instead of printing a size-only "Model:" line and exiting 0 (usability, vllm-server bin)** — `show_model_info` only checked that the path exists, so a non-model directory (or a typo'd model path pointing at e.g. a parent folder) silently produced a `Model: <dirname>` with just `total_size`/`num_files` — no architecture, no hint anything was wrong. It now bails `no config.json found in <path> (not a model directory?)`, matching the actionable messages `model list` (unknown-dir) and `config validate` (bad-config) already emit. 1 unit test. (RIL ISS-142 / TASK-154.)
- **`/health/details` now reports the real readiness and the loaded model id (observability truthfulness, vllm-server)** — `status` was hardcoded `"ok"`, so a draining pod (`mark_not_ready` via SIGTERM/`/shutdown`) kept reporting `ok` while `/health/ready` returned 503 to the orchestrator; it is now derived from the `HealthChecker` (`ok` / `not_ready` / `unhealthy`), and the response gains the loaded `model` id from the tokenizer (absent, not `null`, when the fallback tokenizer names none — the ISS-130 no-null-lie rule). Endpoint HTTP status stays 200 (the body reports readiness); the engine-timeout metric fallback is unchanged. 2 new tests (ready→`ok` + model omission; drained→`not_ready`). (RIL ISS-141 / TASK-153.)
- public-api: vllm-server changed `HealthDetailResponse::prefill_throughput` / `kv_cache_usage_percent` from always-present `Some(0.0)` on engine-timeout to `Option<f32>` omitted-when-`None` (honest "unknown" vs fabricated idle) (RIL ISS-151).
- public-api: vllm-server — `BatchManager` is no longer `Freeze` (gains a manager-owned `AtomicU64` creation counter for deterministic newest-first listing) (RIL ISS-146).
- public-api: vllm-server added `HealthDetailResponse::model` (`Option<String>`, `skip_serializing_if = is_none`) (RIL ISS-141).
- **Whitespace-only input and empty model ids now 400 across every OpenAI endpoint (validation parity, vllm-server)** — embeddings rejected whitespace-only input elements while its comment claimed "every sibling path explicitly forbids" them, but chat message content, completions prompts, and batch prompts only checked `is_empty()` — so a `"   "` prompt/completion/batch-prompt returned 200 and generated from degenerate input where embeddings 400'd the identical value. All four now trim-check (`trim().is_empty()`) before dispatch. Separately, chat + embeddings 400 on `model: ""` while completions and batch silently echoed the blank id back (completions via `response_model`, batch ignored it) — both now 400 `invalid_request_error` via `validate_completion_request_fields` / the batch handler, with `model: None` still allowed as the legacy / server-default. 5 new tests (2 error-contract + 1 chat-integration + 2 batch-handler unit), all RED-first. (RIL ISS-139 / ISS-140 / TASK-152.)
- **`SchedulerStats` removed as fully-disconnected dead code (maintainability, vllm-core)** — the 90-line `scheduler::stats::SchedulerStats` type (9 fields + 4 `record_*` methods, per re-exports plus `Clone`/`Debug`/auto-traits) had ZERO production instantiations in the workspace, while its own doc claimed "Emitted every scheduler step into the metrics channel; used by the Prometheus exporter to render queue-depth and preemption-rate gauges" — nothing emitted it, and no preemption/eviction gauge exists on any wire surface (the real queue-depth / `avg_batch_size` gauges come from the lock-free runtime collector). Removed the module, the `pub mod stats` + `pub use stats::SchedulerStats` re-export, and the stale layout-doc lines. Mirrors the ISS-063 / ISS-127 dead-code-removal precedent. (RIL ISS-138 / TASK-151.)
- public-api: vllm-core removed `scheduler::stats::SchedulerStats` and the `scheduler::stats` module (fully disconnected; no production users) (RIL ISS-138).
- **`active_sequences` / `request_queue_depth` are now live on the CUDA-graph step path (observability, vllm-core)** — with `cuda_graph.enabled` the engine routes exclusively through `select_sequences_for_phase` (graph.rs), which drained the request queue into `running` but never published the two gauges that `build_batch` (state/batch.rs) updates — so `active_sequences` stayed pinned at its init 0 while sequences ran, and `request_queue_depth` never reflected drains. The graph path now calls the same `set_active_sequences(running.len())` / `set_queue_depth(queue.len())` setters, mirroring the regular path. Regression test drives `add_request` + `build_batch_with_graph` and asserts both gauges (RED pre-fix: active_sequences 0). (RIL ISS-136 / TASK-150.)
- **Latency gauges are now honestly labeled "engine step latency" instead of "inference/request latency" (doc-truth, vllm-core)** — `avg_latency_ms` / `latency_p50_ms` / `latency_p90_ms` / `latency_p99_ms` measure ONE engine scheduler step (`record_latency` fires once per step at scheduler/batch.rs, spec_dispatch/dispatch.rs, engine/graph_step.rs), NOT end-to-end request latency or TTFT — but the Prometheus HELP text, `MetricsSnapshot` field docs, the tutorial metric list, and the Grafana dashboard's "Request Latency" panel all labeled them as request/inference latency, so operators read step time as request time. All four surfaces now say engine step latency; a regression test pins the honest HELP so the mislabel can't return. Metric NAMES are unchanged (no dashboard/query breakage). (RIL ISS-134 / TASK-149.)
- **Engine step errors now surface on the primary observability surfaces (`errors_total` on `/metrics` Prometheus + OTLP, observability, vllm-core)** — `errors_total` (the production counter behind `record_engine_error`, engine/run.rs) was written and exposed only on the admin-gated `/debug/metrics`, so operators scraping `/metrics` or exporting via OTLP saw zero step-error signal while the engine silently failed steps. The Prometheus exporter now renders `errors_total` with the live counter value, and the OTLP `SCHEMA_MAP` gains the `engine.errors` instrument — the parity test (`metric_schema_mapping_covers_all_exported_prometheus_metrics`) pins both surfaces in lockstep, and a new live-value test drives 2 errors through to the `errors_total 2` wire line. Doc: `docs/tutorial/05-production.md` metric list now lists it. (RIL ISS-133 / TASK-148.)
- **`vllm-server` now fails fast when an explicitly-requested config can't be honored instead of silently booting on defaults (usability + correctness, vllm-server)** — passing `--config prod.yaml` (or setting `$VLLM_CONFIG_PATH`) with a missing, unreadable, or unparseable file previously `WARN`-logged and fell back to built-in defaults, booting a healthy-looking server on the WRONG settings — the operator's deliberate port/host/engine tuning silently vanished. `AppConfig::load` now returns a typed `ConfigLoadError` and the CLI folds it into startup: `vllm-server` prints `config error: <path and reason>` to stderr and exits 78 (the config-error family) instead of serving. No explicit config source still defaults cleanly; unknown top-level keys remain `WARN`-only (RIL ISS-097). Reverses TASK-107's "degrade to defaults" leg (DEC-057); the missing/malformed config tests flip from defaults to fail-fast. (RIL ISS-132 / TASK-147 / DEC-057.)
- **public-api: vllm-server changed** — `AppConfig::load` now returns `Result<AppConfig, ConfigLoadError>`; `CliArgs::to_app_config` returns `Result<AppConfig, ConfigLoadError>`; new public `config::ConfigLoadError` enum (`Missing`/`Unreadable`/`Parse`) (RIL ISS-132 / DEC-057).
- **OpenAI wire contract: `/v1/models/{id}` returns the bare model object, chat messages omit the `"name": null` lie, and embeddings report true input-token usage (API contract, vllm-server)** — (a) `GET /v1/models/{id}` returned the model wrapped in a `{object:"list",data:[...]}` envelope, breaking OpenAI SDK `client.models.retrieve(id)` which parses `object` against `"model"` and reads top-level `id` — it now returns the bare `{id, object:"model", created, owned_by, ...}` object (RIL ISS-129); (b) every chat response message serialized a `"name": null` key OpenAI never emits, since `ChatMessage.name` lacked `skip_serializing_if` and is always unset in responses — it is now omitted when `None`, while requests still parse a present `name` (RIL ISS-130); (c) embeddings `usage.prompt_tokens` was the Σ of embedding-vector dimensions (a 4-dim model reported 4× the true tokens), not the input token count — the handler now threads the true encoded-length sum into the response (RIL ISS-131). Tests: by-id handler test updated to the bare-object shape + no-list-envelope, wire-level no-null-name assertion on a live non-streaming chat response, and embeddings-usage unit tests pinning `prompt_tokens` = input tokens. (RIL ISS-129 / ISS-130 / ISS-131 / TASK-146.)
- **public-api: vllm-server changed** — `EmbeddingsResponse::new` now takes an explicit `prompt_tokens: usize` (was: derived from embedding-vector lengths) so `usage.prompt_tokens` reports true input tokens (RIL ISS-131).
- **Chat SSE streaming now reports one stream-start `created` on every chunk, matching its own documented contract (API contract, vllm-server)** — `ChatChunk`'s `created` doc promises "Unix timestamp at the start of the stream", but `ChatChunk::new` / `new_usage_chunk` stamped a fresh `unix_now_secs()` per chunk, so a finish or `include_usage` chunk could drift from the canonical value already captured once at stream start (the n > 1 consolidated chunk, RIL ISS-122) across a one-second boundary — the exact class of per-chunk divergence ISS-122 eliminated from the completions path. Both constructors now REQUIRE an explicit `created: i64` (no call site can reintroduce per-chunk timestamps), and the n = 1 chat path captures one stream-epoch value just like the n > 1 path. 1 unit test pins that the constructors carry the supplied value verbatim; 1 integration test walks a live n = 1 stream and asserts every JSON chunk's `created` is identical. (RIL ISS-123 / TASK-145.)
- **public-api: vllm-server changed** — `ChatChunk::new` / `ChatChunk::new_usage_chunk` now take an explicit `created: i64` (was: stamped a fresh per-chunk `unix_now_secs()`) to enforce the struct's documented stream-start timestamp contract on every chat SSE chunk (RIL ISS-123).
- **Chat streaming no longer stamps `role: "assistant"` on every delta — only the first chunk carries it, matching the OpenAI wire contract (API contract, vllm-server)** — the n > 1 streaming path already emitted `role` only on each candidate's first event via `first_emitted`, but the single-shot (`n = 1`) path stamped `"assistant"` on **every** chunk (4 `ChatChunk` construction sites: skipped-token, per-token, flushed-tail, finish). OpenAI's streaming spec emits `role` once per stream; strict wire-diffing clients and byte-budget-conscious consumers flagged the repeated field. The n = 1 unfold now carries a `role_emitted` flag (seventh state tuple element): the first delta gets `"assistant"`, every later delta gets an empty role — including the finish chunk, which still gets `"assistant"` on a zero-token stream (nothing else preceded it). The **n > 1 path had the inverse gap**: a candidate that finalised with ZERO token chunks never emitted a role marker (its intermediate-finish delta and its consolidated-final delta both carried `role: ""`), so clients that initialise a choice's author from the first delta saw no author for that choice. Both n > 1 finish sites now mark the role when `first_emitted[index]` is unset — the intermediate-finish chunk carries `"assistant"` and flips the flag so the consolidated chunk does not repeat it; a zero-token candidate whose finish itself triggers `all_done` carries `"assistant"` in the consolidated per-index delta. 2 regression integration tests (n=1 chronological role + n>1 zero-token candidates each get a marker). (RIL ISS-126.)
- **`POST /v1/batches` now answers `202 Accepted`, not `200 OK`, matching OpenAI's spec and vLLM's own endpoint (API contract, vllm-server)** — OpenAI documents `POST /v1/batches` as returning the batch object with `202 Accepted` (the job is queued for asynchronous execution, not completed synchronously); the pre-fix contract returned a plain `200`, so strict SDK clients that branch on the status code (e.g. checking `202` before polling `GET /v1/batches/{id}`) treated the response as an error. The handler returns `(202, BatchResponse)` now; the body is unchanged. 2 unit tests assert the `202`. (RIL ISS-124.)
- **Unmatched routes answer `404` with the OpenAI `{error: ...}` envelope instead of axum's plain-text `Not Found` (usability, vllm-server)** — a typo'd route (client bug, SDK pointed at the wrong base URL, `curl` miss) hit axum's default fallback, whose plain-text body fails the JSON decoding every OpenAI SDK performs on non-2xx responses — surfacing as an opaque client-side parse error instead of an actionable `error.message`. `build_app` now registers a router-level `.fallback` returning `404` + `{"error":{"message":"Not Found","type":"invalid_request_error"}}`; real routes are unaffected. 2 integration tests drive the production router through `tower::ServiceExt::oneshot` (envelope on unmatched path; `/v1/models` still dispatches normally). (RIL ISS-125.)
- **Every SSE chunk now carries `model` + `created`, so strict parsers (schema-validating clients, chat/`completions` diff tools) accept the streams (API contract, vllm-server)** — an SSE-wire audit (RIL ISS-122) found schema drift between the streaming and non-streaming paths: the single-shot **completions** chunks (`n = 1` and `n > 1`) omitted `model` and `created` entirely, and the `n > 1` chat consolidated final chunk omitted `created` only. `vLLM-lite`'s own `ChatChunk`/`CompletionResponse` types serialize those fields on the non-streaming responses, so a client parsing both paths with one schema hit a missing key exactly at the end of a stream. Both completions paths now stamp `model` (the resolved request model) + one `created` epoch captured at stream start on **every** chunk (n > 1 already echoed `model` pre-fix; n = 1's four `json!` sites are now consistent), and the `n > 1` chat consolidated chunk carries the same stream-epoch `created` (`ChatChunk::new`/`new_usage_chunk` still take their own per-chunk now-timestamps — tracked as RIL ISS-123). The pinned skipped-token chunk wire-shape test now documents the full `{id, object, model, created, choices}` contract. (RIL ISS-122 / TASK-144.)
- **n > 1 streaming no longer drops the last-finishing candidate's split multi-byte tail (correctness, vllm-server + vllm-model)** — each candidate's `Finished` event flushes its `StreamingDecoder` tail (ISS-105) into a local `tail`, but when that finish made `all_done` true the consolidated chunk **re-flushed** every candidate — `flush()` clears `pending` (tokenizer.rs), so the just-flushed last candidate returned `""` and its held-back bytes (a CJK/emoji char split across the final tokens) were lost exactly at stream end. The chat and completions `all_done` branches now use the already-flushed `tail` for the triggering candidate and only re-flush the others. New pub `Tokenizer::from_hf_tokenizer` (in-memory HF tokenizer seam, RIL ISS-121) + a regression integration test driving `n = 2` through a byte-level BPE that splits 你 (E4 BD A0) across three tokens — fails pre-fix, passes post-fix. (RIL ISS-121 / TASK-143.)
- **`vllm-server --version` / `vllm --version` now report the real Cargo version instead of a hardcoded `0.1.0` (maintainability, vllm-server)** — both clap commands carried `#[command(version = "0.1.0")]`, duplicating `[workspace.package] version`; the instant the crate version is bumped (a real semver release), `--version` would silently keep advertising the stale value while the release manifest derives the new tag. Both now compile `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` (the single source of truth; `vllm-server` via a `SERVER_VERSION` const, `vllm` inline), so `--version` tracks the release for operators and support. 2 parse tests guard that the rendered version contains `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`. (RIL ISS-119 / TASK-141.)
- **Auth rejections no longer break OpenAI SDK error typing: 401/429 now carry the error envelope (API contract, vllm-server)** — the auth middleware returned an **empty body** for `401 Unauthorized` (bad/missing key) and `429 Too Many Requests` (token bucket exhausted), and a **bare `{"error":"..."}` string** for its defensive `413` oversize-body path. OpenAI SDKs parse `error.message`/`type`/`code`; an empty body raised a generic/`None` error clients could not distinguish. All three now emit the standard `{error:{message,type,code}}` envelope — `401` → `invalid_request_error` + `invalid_api_key`, `429` → `rate_limit_exceeded`, `413` (both the auth defensive branch and the `RequestBodyLimitLayer` whose `text/plain "length limit exceeded"` body was the **actual** production 413, since the limit fires above auth) → `invalid_request_error` + `request_too_large`. 2 new integration tests + 1 new body-limit wiring test. (RIL ISS-118 / TASK-140.)
- **`GET /v1/batches` returns the OpenAI list envelope instead of a bare array (API contract, vllm-server)** — the list endpoint serialized `Vec<BatchResponse>` directly, so OpenAI SDKs that read `parsed["data"]` broke (sibling `/v1/models` already used `{"object":"list",...}`). It now returns `{"object":"list","data":[...]}`, and `GET /v1/models` (+ `GET /v1/models/{id}`) additionally send `cache-control: no-cache` so proxies don't serve a stale model list after a model is removed. 2 tests (handler unit + models integration). (RIL ISS-118 / TASK-140.)
- **The `vllm` CLI's arguments match what its usage docs promise (usability, vllm-server bin)** — the tool's own doc comment showed positional invocation (`vllm config validate config.yaml`, `vllm model list /models`, `vllm model info /models/llama-7b`) but every argument was an `#[arg(long)]` flag (`--file`/`--dir`/`--path`), so the documented commands failed with `error: unexpected argument ... found`. The three arguments are now positional with help text, matching the sole documentation; 3 clap parse tests guard the forms. (RIL ISS-117 / TASK-139.)
- **Every CLI flag is now documented in `--help` (usability, vllm-server)** — six flags rendered with a blank help section (`--model`, `--api-key`, `--api-key-file`, `--insecure-allow-public-no-auth`, `--log-dir`, `--config`), so a fresh operator reading `--help` learned nothing about the model-path requirement, API-key sources, the SEC-01 escape hatch, JSON file logging, or the YAML config flag. Each gained `help = ...` (plus clap's `env:` metadata). A regression test walks the flattened clap `Command` and asserts every arg except `--help`/`--version` has non-empty help. Also backticks `cli::args` in a doc comment (clippy `doc_markdown`), restoring the zero-pedantic-warnings state. (RIL ISS-115 / TASK-137.)
- **A full annotated `config/example.yaml` ships with the server, drift-guarded by CI (docs, vllm-server)** — the `--config` flag reads YAML but the only shipped example was a minimal README snippet missing the non-obvious sections (`cors`, `server.multi_node`, `engine.engine_mailbox_capacity`, `engine.max_model_len`, `engine.draft_specs`, `auth.rate_limit_overrides`, `observability`). The new `config/example.yaml` documents every `AppConfig` section with annotations and the precedence contract, and a `config/tests.rs` test loads it (via `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`) and asserts `AppConfig::validate()` passes plus spot-checks the hard-to-guess fields — schema drift now fails CI instead of silently shipping a stale reference. (RIL ISS-116 / TASK-138.)
- **SSE streams now keep the connection alive across idle gaps, so proxies don't drop long-running generations (usability, vllm-server)** — the chat and completions SSE streams emitted only `data:` events: a multi-second prefill or a reasoning-model pause before the first token left the connection silent, and any proxy/load-balancer with an inactivity timeout (nginx `proxy_read_timeout`, Cloudflare) could kill the stream mid-generation. All four `Sse::new` response sites (chat n=1 + n>1, completions n=1 + n>1) now wrap with axum's `Sse::keep_alive(KeepAlive::default())` — a standards-compliant empty SSE comment every 15 seconds of **idle** (the keepalive timer resets on every real event and forwards stream end immediately, so `[DONE]` is never delayed and strict clients see no spurious data). Covered by the existing streaming/framing tests which consume full SSE bodies. (RIL ISS-113 / TASK-135.)
- **`POST /v1/batches` no longer under-charges its rate-limit budget ~820× by deferring compute behind the 100k cost cap (security, vllm-server)** — `estimate_request_cost`'s batch branch clamped its charge to `[1.0, 100_000]` like the synchronous endpoints, but a batch is **deferred compute**: every prompt runs to completion as its own generation in the background worker (`MAX_BATCH_PROMPTS = 10_000`, per-prompt `max_tokens` ceiling 8192 ≈ 82M token-equivalents), so a single batch request was charged at most 100_000 while queuing up to ~820× that of work — a cost-based rate limit could be drained once and then queue a huge backlog of batch jobs. The batch branch now charges `max_tokens × N + prompt_words` with no upper clamp (floor 1.0 only), so the charge scales with the deferred work and the bucket budget admits only what it can afford. 1 regression test (a 2000-prompt × `max_tokens=100` batch costs 202_000, not the pre-fix 100_000). (RIL ISS-112 / TASK-134.)
- **A streamed/response-channel client disconnect now cancels the sequence instead of generating the whole `max_tokens` budget into a dead channel (correctness, vllm-core)** — on every step path the engine sends each sampled token into the 64-slot response channel with `try_send`; when the receiver had been dropped (client gone / connection aborted mid-stream — the engine's **only** disconnect signal on non-streaming handlers), `try_send` returned `TrySendError::Closed` and the code treated it as a no-op, so the sequence burned the rest of its token budget on a channel nobody reads, holding KV blocks and the `requests_in_flight` balance. All three step paths — regular (`scheduler/batch.rs`), speculative (`spec_dispatch/dispatch.rs`), CUDA-Graph (`graph_step.rs`) — now collect the `Closed` sequences and cancel them (via the new `Engine::cancel_on_closed_channels`, after the per-sequence scheduler fold) so KV is released and in-flight accounting balances immediately; `Full` (slow consumer) still logs + counts instead. 2 regression tests (engine-level regular + speculative). (RIL ISS-110 / TASK-132.)
- **`stream_options.include_usage` now works: the chat stream emits the OpenAI usage chunk before `[DONE]` (wire contract, vllm-server)** — `StreamOptions` and `ChatChunk.usage` were documented on the request type but nothing read them, so a client sending `stream_options: {include_usage: true}` got the field silently dropped and never saw token usage on the stream. The `n == 1` chat SSE state machine carries a completion-token counter and routes `Streaming → EmitUsage → EmitDoneSentinel` when `include_usage` is set: one extra final chunk with `choices: []` and the real `usage {prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, total_tokens}`, emitted AFTER the `finish_reason` chunk and BEFORE `[DONE]` (the OpenAI streaming-usage contract). The `n > 1` parallel path honors it too — the consolidated final chunk (all N choices) is followed by the same `choices: []` usage chunk, with `completion_tokens` aggregated across **all** choices (OpenAI bills every output). Pre-fix `n == 1` was implemented but `n > 1` silently dropped the field; both now covered by integration tests. (RIL ISS-111 / TASK-133 + RIL ISS-114 / TASK-136.)
- **Server unit tests that touch `VLLM_*` env vars no longer flake under `cargo test` (test-quality, vllm-server)** — `cargo test` (single process, many threads) raced a `cli/args/tests.rs` test parsing clap args declared `env = "VLLM_PORT"` / `"VLLM_HOST"` / ... (which read the process env at parse time) against a sibling test's `set_var` (`config/tests.rs` writing `VLLM_CONFIG_PATH`) — `test_to_app_config_preserves_yaml_values` failed intermittently and passed in isolation. `nextest` (per-process) was immune, so CI stayed green while `cargo test` users hit flakes. A single crate-wide `test_fixtures::ENV_TEST_LOCK` now gates every env-touching server unit test (`config/tests.rs` + all 33 `cli/args` tests). Verified under the flake scenario: `cargo test -p vllm-server --lib` (517 tests, single process, threaded) fully green. (RIL ISS-109 / TASK-130.)
- **Setting a documented-but-inert engine knob now warns at startup instead of silently doing nothing (usability, vllm-server)** — `engine.tensor_parallel_size` (claims sharding only the inert multi-node / vllm-dist path could deliver; this build runs a single model worker) and `engine.max_waiting_batches` (claims waiting-queue backpressure the scheduler does not apply) were parsed, validated, and stored but consumed by nothing — an operator configuring either got a silent no-op. `bootstrap::engine::inert_engine_knob_warnings` now returns (and main.rs logs, once, at startup, only for **non-default** values) a `WARN` naming the knob and why it changes nothing — the same config-honesty pattern as the ISS-097 unknown-key WARN. 4 unit tests. (RIL ISS-091 / TASK-131.)
- **The OTLP exporter now streams the full `/metrics` surface instead of five always-0 instruments (observability, vllm-core)** — with the `opentelemetry` feature on, `OtlpExporter` read only the collector's atomic fields + draft counters, so every headline engine metric that lives on `/metrics` (tokens_total, avg/p50/p90/p99 latency, kv_cache_usage_percent, prefix_cache_hit_rate, prefill/decode_throughput_tps, avg/current_batch_size, requests_in_flight, avg_scheduler_wait_time_ms, dropped_tokens_total) was absent from OTLP — an SRE dashboarding Jaeger/Datadog/Honeycomb saw nothing while Prometheus scraped it all; and the schema map also carried five fabricated always-0 instruments with no writers anywhere (`gpu_memory_used/total_bytes` — no GPU-memory source; `is_leader` — no leader-election in production; `stale scheduler_queue_size`/`inflight_requests` — no writers, superseded by the live `request_queue_depth`/`requests_in_flight`). `SCHEMA_MAP` now sources each tick from the lock-free runtime snapshot too (29 entries = the exact PrometheusExporter surface, confirmed by a parity test that parses the exporter's live output), and the five fabricated instruments are removed instead of exported as zeros forever. ADR-021 updated. (RIL ISS-103 / TASK-129.)
- **`/metrics` snapshot is no longer destructive — concurrent scrapes can't steal each other's samples (correctness, vllm-core)** — `LockFreeMetrics::snapshot()` drained the bounded latency / batch-size / scheduler-wait ring channels with `try_recv`, so the first consumer to read reset the "recent window" and any second consumer (the engine's `GetMetrics` round-trip serving `/health/details` and `/debug/kv-cache`, a concurrent `/metrics` scrape, or a future OTLP exporter) saw zeros or leftovers — record a latency, snapshot, snapshot again and the second read returned `avg 0.0`. The rings become bounded `parking_lot::Mutex<VecDeque>` rolling windows (oldest evicted at capacity; producers take an uncontended short lock); `snapshot()` now clones without draining, so any number of consumers observe identical samples. `parking_lot` is now a non-optional `vllm-core` dependency (it was gated behind `multi-node`). 1 regression test (a sample set must survive two snapshot reads). (RIL ISS-107 / TASK-127.)
- **Duplicate speculative gauge removed: `speculative_acceptance_rate` is the single accepted/drafted wire name (correctness, vllm-core)** — `speculative_acceptance_rate` and `speculative_efficiency` were fed the identical accepted/drafted ratio at the sole producer and exported with the same HELP text, so `/metrics` (and OTLP) emitted two differently-named gauges that always carried the same number — dashboards keyed on one tracked a byte-for-byte copy of the other. `speculative_efficiency` is removed end-to-end (collector field/`get_gauge` arm/writer, dispatch producer, Prometheus exporter, OTLP `SCHEMA_MAP`). 1 new wire-contract test (duplicate name absent from the export). (RIL ISS-108 / TASK-128.)
- **Streamed SSE output no longer corrupts multi-byte UTF-8 (correctness, vllm-model + vllm-server)** — for byte-level BPE tokenizers (the shipped Qwen defaults) the streaming paths decoded each sampled token in isolation, and a single Unicode char split across two consecutive byte-merges lossy-decoded to `U+FFFD` (`�`) mid-stream while the non-streaming full-list decode was correct — streamed CJK/emoji rendered as mojibake, reproduced empirically with a byte-level BPE holding 你 (E4 BD A0) as three separate tokens (whole-list `="你"`, per-token `="�"`). A new `StreamingDecoder` (vllm-model) keeps a pending token window and emits only the longest prefix whose decode does not end in a lossy `U+FFFD`, holding the tail for the next token, and `flush()` recovers the remainder at stream end — tokenizer-agnostic, deterministic, and it survives a *genuine* model-emitted replacement char. Every chat/completions stream (n=1 unfold, n>1 parallel) now owns a per-stream/per-candidate decoder, and finish events flush the held-back tail so partial bytes are not dropped at channel close (completions `parallel_finish_choice` now carries the flushed tail text, preserving the ISS-047 `text`-field contract). 3 model + streaming-path server tests. (RIL ISS-105 / TASK-126.)
- **`GET /v1/models/{id}` now exists and matches the list endpoint (missing-route fix, vllm-server)** — the server registered only `/v1/models` (the list), so SDKs that resolve a model id after listing it (`GET /v1/models/canonical-id`) hit axum's default empty 404 body instead of a model payload. The id advertised by the list endpoint (the served model name from `Tokenizer::model_name()`) now resolves to the same single-element `ModelObject`, and unknown ids return a clean OpenAI `404 model_not_found` (`invalid_request_error`) body. 2 integration tests. (RIL ISS-106 / TASK-125.)
- **OpenAI POST handlers return OpenAI-format error bodies instead of axum's plain-text 422/415 (API contract, vllm-server)** — axum's default `Json<T>` rejection returned `422 Unprocessable Entity` with a plain-text body (`Failed to deserialize the JSON body into the target type: ...`) for wrong-shaped-but-valid JSON, `415` for a missing `Content-Type`, leaking serde internals into the response; OpenAI clients parse `{error: {message, type, code}}` and treat non-400 as opaque, so client error handling broke. A shared `OpenaiJson<T>` extractor now maps every rejection onto the handlers' own OpenAI-format `ErrorResponse`: `400 invalid_request_error` for syntax + shape-mismatch (serde detail kept but stripped of axum's "into the target type" wrapper), `415` for a missing/wrong `Content-Type`. All OpenAI POST handlers (chat, completions, embeddings, create_batch) use it. 5 extractor unit tests + json_schema-rejection contract test updated (now 400). Public API +new `OpenaiJson`. (RIL ISS-104 / TASK-124.)
- **`GET /v1/models` no longer reports a stale hardcoded `created` epoch (honesty, vllm-server)** — every model object returned `created: 1_700_000_000` (2023-11-14) regardless of the model or deployment, so clients displaying model creation dates always saw the same fixed value. `created` now reports when the server instance serves the model entry (`unix_now_secs()`, vLLM's convention). 1 regression assertion extended. (RIL ISS-098 / TASK-118.)
- **A typo'd/unknown top-level config key now logs a WARN instead of silently degrading to defaults (usability, vllm-server)** — every `AppConfig` section carries `#[serde(default)]`, so a config with `engin:` instead of `engine:` (or any misspelled or retired section) parsed cleanly and the server ran on built-in defaults with **zero signal**. `AppConfig::load` now re-parses the raw YAML as JSON (`unknown_top_level_keys`) and emits a `WARN` naming the key plus the recognised sections (`server`, `engine`, `auth`, `cors`, `observability` when the `opentelemetry` feature is on) — the lenient parse stays authoritative, so existing configs are unaffected. Best-effort: exotic YAML that doesn't map to JSON simply skips the check. 4 new unit tests. (RIL ISS-097 / TASK-117.)
- **`prefill_throughput_tps` / `decode_throughput_tps` are now live on `/metrics` (observability, vllm-core)** — the lifetime prefill/decode token counters fed these gauges, but their only writers (`record_prefill_tokens` / `record_decode_tokens`) lived under `#[cfg(test)]`, so both gauges were pinned at `0.000` on every scrape while the model clearly generated. They are now production-callable and every step path that composes a `Batch` (regular `scheduler/batch.rs`, speculative `spec_dispatch`, CUDA-graph `graph_step`) records the phase split via the new `EnhancedMetricsCollector::record_batch_phase_tokens(&Batch)` — prefill tokens = Σ input-token lengths of prefill sequences (the vLLM convention: tokens *processed* in the prefill phase), decode tokens = number of decode sequences (one token each). `tokens_total` (emitted tokens, ISS-083) is untouched — the two gauges measure processing vs emission and are distinct. 2 new tests (collector-level phase-split + engine-level liveness regression; the latter fails pre-fix at 0.0). Public API +1. (RIL ISS-095 / TASK-115.)
- **`avg_scheduler_wait_time_ms` is now live on `/metrics` (observability, vllm-core)** — the gauge exported by Prometheus was pinned at `0.000` in production: `RecordQueue` tracks every queued request's arrival instant, but the only recorder (`record_scheduler_wait_time`) lived under `#[cfg(test)]`, so no production path ever fed the bounded ring channel (the famous "Average scheduler wait = 0 under load" dashboard). Both scheduler batch builders (`build_batch` on the regular/speculative paths and `select_sequences_for_phase` on the CUDA-Graph path) now drain the request queue with each sequence's queue→admission wait and record it, closing the documented-follow-up left by the ISS-095 throughput fix. `RequestQueue::drain_by_phase` now returns each drained sequence paired with its elapsed wait (`Vec<(Sequence, Duration)>`); the recorder and its collector forwarder are production code. 3 new/strengthened tests (collector round-trip, 3-sample average, engine-level liveness with a deterministic 10 ms sleep — fails pre-fix at `0.0`). Public API: `record_scheduler_wait_time` +1, `drain_by_phase` signature change. (RIL TASK-119.)
- **Speculative decoding no longer drops tokens silently when the client channel is full (observability, vllm-core)** — `emit_verified_tokens` did `let _ = try_send(...)` and swallowed `TrySendError::Full`, so on the speculative path (which bursts several accepted drafts per step) a consumer that drained slower than generation lost tokens from the stream with **no warning and no counter** — the exact overload the regular decode path logs and counts in `dropped_tokens_total` (ISS-074). The speculative emission path now mirrors the regular `try_send_token` contract: `Full` logs a `WARN` and increments `dropped_tokens_total`, `Closed` stays a silent no-op. 1 regression test (fails pre-fix at `dropped_tokens_total == 0`). (RIL TASK-120.)
- **`/metrics` `kv_cache_usage_percent` is now live without a `GetMetrics` round-trip (observability, vllm-core)** — the sole production writer (`record_kv_cache_usage`) lived inside the engine's `GetMetrics` message arm, but the `/metrics` handler renders the runtime snapshot directly: an operator scraping only `/metrics` saw the gauge pinned at `0.000` forever while `/health/details` / `/debug/kv-cache` (which do round-trip) reported the real value — same semantic, stale surface. Every `Engine::step` (regular, speculative, and the CUDA-graph `step_with_graph`) now records allocator usage via `record_step_metrics` (O(1) counters), on success and error alike. 1 regression test: a 16-token prefill step leaves `kv_cache_usage_percent > 0` with no `GetMetrics` sent (fails pre-fix at 0). (RIL ISS-100 / TASK-121.)
- **`/debug/metrics` no longer fabricates a permanent `packing_sequences_total: 0` counter (observability, vllm-server)** — `get_counter` has no storage arm for that name and no writer exists anywhere (sequence packing is inert per DEC-054), so the snapshot pinned an empty value that could never change — the same fabricated-zero class as the ISS-090 `errors_total` and ISS-093 `spans_active` removals. The counter list is extracted to `snapshot_counter_entries()` with a regression test asserting absence. (RIL ISS-101 / TASK-122.)
- **Monitoring/deploy artifacts no longer point at a dead `9090` metrics port (ops, vllm-server)** — `/metrics` is served on the main HTTP server port (8000); `PrometheusExporter` stores a port but never binds one, so port 9090 did not exist anywhere while several committed artifacts referenced it. `config/prometheus.yml` targeted `vllm:9090` (compose service is actually `vllm-server`, only publishes 8000) so the Prometheus target was permanently DOWN; the plain k8s manifests declared a dead `containerPort 9090` / service mapping, a fabricated `metrics:{port:9090}` configmap section (which `AppConfig` does not recognise — ISS-097 WARN), and never set `VLLM_MODEL` (server cannot start) or pointed at the configmap. All corrected (`vllm-server:8000` target; configmap now loaded via `VLLM_CONFIG_PATH`; deployment sets `VLLM_MODEL` + mounts the model dir), and the tutorial's invented metric names (`vllm:request_total`, `vllm:request_duration_seconds`, `vllm:kv_cache_utilization`) were replaced with the real exporter names. 1 regression test. (RIL ISS-102 / TASK-123.)
- **`/v1/completions` no longer reports a literal `"default"` model (honesty, vllm-server)** — when the legacy endpoint's optional `model` is omitted, the response previously echoed `model: "default"` (a placeholder indistinguishable from a real id, breaking clients that key routing/billing off `response.model`). The response now reports the model actually serving (`Tokenizer::model_name()`, the same source as `GET /v1/models`; `"unknown"` only when even the tokenizer has no name). A client-provided `model` is still echoed. 2 new unit tests. (RIL ISS-094.)
- **`--config` YAML values are no longer clobbered by CLI defaults; the documented adaptive-speculative default is no longer inverted (usability, vllm-server)** — every CLI arg carried a hardcoded clap `default_value`, so `to_app_config` unconditionally overwrote the values just parsed from `--config` / `VLLM_CONFIG_PATH`: an operator's `kv_blocks: 4096` was silently reset to 1024, and `enable_adaptive_speculative` was forced to `false` (inverting the documented config default `true`). The clobbering args are now `Option<T>` and `to_app_config` overrides only what the operator explicitly set via flag/env. Precedence contract: **CLI/env > `--config` YAML > built-in defaults**. `--help` documents each knob's effective default. 4 new unit regressions + 2 process-isolated env-precedence integration tests. (RIL ISS-081 / TASK-106.)
- **`vllm config validate` now agrees with the running server (usability, vllm-server bin)** — the tool hand-rolled stricter rules than the server: it required `server`/`engine` sections the server's `AppConfig` defaults, and missed the port / `num_kv_blocks` upper bounds the server enforces, so a config the server accepts was rejected and vice-versa. It now parses with the same `AppConfig` schema and runs the server's real `validate()`. `vllm model info` also shows the checkpoint's `eos_token_id`. (RIL TASK-110.)
- **A config file that *exists* but fails to read/parse now logs a WARN instead of silently falling back to defaults (usability, vllm-server)** — a typo'd YAML quietly degraded to built-in defaults with no signal. `AppConfig::load` now surfaces read/parse failures via `load_config_file`; missing files stay silent (`--config` is optional). Also: the missing-`--model` usage line no longer renders `--allow-stub` as an alternative to `--model`. (RIL TASK-107.)
- **Generation now stops at the model's end-of-sentence token, reporting `finish_reason=stop` instead of burning the full `max_tokens` budget (correctness, vllm-core + vllm-server)** — the engine never compared sampled tokens against a model EOS id, so every 'short' answer ran to `max_tokens` and reported `Length`. `Engine` gains `eos_token_id` (public field + `set_eos_token_id` setter) and `finalize_stop_sequences` (the shared finalize seam for the regular / speculative / CUDA-graph step paths) now finalizes with `FinishReason::Stop` when the last generated token equals the model EOS id. The server wires the id from the checkpoint `config.json` (`configure_eos`, single-int or list form) after construction. The EOS token itself is still streamed (consistent with stop-sequence behavior); excluding the control token from output is a documented follow-up. 2 engine tests + 3 parser tests. (RIL ISS-075 / TASK-109.)
- **`requests_in_flight` is now a live gauge, and its decrement can no longer wrap underflow (observability)** — the Prometheus `requests_in_flight` gauge and OTLP `inflight_requests` were pinned at **0** under any load: the only writers (`record_request_start` / `record_request_end`) lived under `#[cfg(test)]` and no production engine/scheduler call touched them. The counter is now wired into the request lifecycle — `Engine::add_request` increments it for admitted (`seq_id != 0`) requests and `Engine::finalize_finished` decrements it for every Stop/Length/Cancelled finalization — and the decrement is a **saturating** `fetch_update` (the pre-existing raw `fetch_sub(1)` would have wrapped to `u64::MAX` ≈ 1.8e19 on the first unbalanced end). 4 new tests (live-and-balanced round trip, rejected admission not counted, cancel balances, end saturates at 0). (RIL ISS-082 / TASK-096.)
- **`tokens_total` ("Total tokens generated") now counts generated tokens on every step path, consistently** — the metric is exported by the Prometheus `/metrics` and documented as "Total tokens generated", but the regular (`scheduler/batch.rs`) and speculative (`spec_dispatch/dispatch.rs`) step paths recorded the sum of per-sequence **input** token lengths — the full prompt length on every prefill step — while only the CUDA-graph path counted the emitted **output** results (`results.len()`). A 40-token prompt with `max_tokens = 5` therefore accumulated ~44 "generated" tokens on the regular/speculative paths and 5 on the CUDA-graph path, inflating throughput dashboards and making the same counter measure different units depending on the build/path. All three step paths now record the emitted (output) token count. 1 new engine-level regression test (40-token prompt + `max_tokens=5` ⇒ exactly 5, was ~44). (RIL ISS-083 / TASK-095.)
- **API keys from `--api-key-file` / `VLLM_API_KEYS_FILE` / `api_keys_env` are now actually enforced (security)** — `resolve_api_keys()` merges all three documented key sources (inline, env var, file), but the production binary constructed the auth middleware from the **inline `api_keys` list only**. An operator using the file/env source got a non-empty resolved-key set — so the SEC-01 startup warning did **not** fire — while `auth_middleware` was `None` and the **inference API ran completely unauthenticated** (only `/debug/*` and `/shutdown` stayed fail-closed via `require_admin`). The middleware is now built from the resolved all-source key set via the new `vllm_server::app::build_auth_middleware`, so enforcement matches the documented/auth-posture key set. 3 new unit tests (file-only keys → middleware present with those keys, inline-only, none). (RIL ISS-080 / TASK-094.)
- **`/debug/metrics` no longer inflates fixed-point ratio gauges 100×** — the sampling ratio gauges are stored ×100_000 (`record_packing_efficiency`, `record_speculative_acceptance`), and the Prometheus exporter correctly restores them by dividing by 100_000, but the JSON admin snapshot divided by **1000** — so `record_packing_efficiency(0.85)` read `0.850` on `/metrics` and `85.000` on `/debug/metrics` (two orders of magnitude apart on the "same" metric). The snapshot now uses a shared `fixed_ratio_to_f64` helper with the ×100_000 scale. 1 new unit test (fails pre-fix: 85.0 vs 0.85). (RIL ISS-079 / TASK-093.)
- **`temperature = 0` now forces greedy argmax even when `top_p < 1.0` (deterministic-reproduction contract)** — `sample_one_with_params` and the legacy `sample_batch` checked the `top_p < 1.0` branch **before** `temperature`, so a request with `temperature = 0` + `top_p < 1` (a common client combination — both params are forwarded verbatim by the HTTP layer) ran `top_p_sample` on the **raw, un-scaled** logits with a seeded/thread random threshold: a "greedy" request drew from the RNG, was non-reproducible even with a `seed`, and could emit tokens the argmax would never pick. This contradicted the unconditional `SamplingParams::temperature` doc ("`0.0` selects greedy argmax") and the seed doc's "greedy paths bypass the RNG entirely". The branch order is now `temperature <= 0` ⇒ greedy first, then `top_p < 1` ⇒ nucleus, else temperature sampling, and the logprob computation follows the same precedence so `logprob` reflects the actual (greedy ⇒ full-softmax) distribution. 1 new regression test spanning 256 seeds (fails pre-fix on the two-way-argmax tie). (RIL ISS-078 / TASK-092.)
- **Empty `Batch.sampling_params` now degrades to greedy decoding per sequence, as documented (synthetic-batch stall)** — `sample_batch_with_params` iterated `logits_list.zip(params_list).zip(seen_tokens)`, so a batch carrying real per-sequence logits but an **empty** `sampling_params` (the synthetic-test-batch shape the `Batch` docs call "equivalent to greedy decoding") produced **zero** sampled tokens — the engine emitted nothing for that batch and every sequence silently stalled (no advance, KV blocks pinned by `update()`'s no-op). The function now samples per `logits_list` row and falls back to `SamplingParams::default()` (greedy) / an empty seen-set for missing `params`/`seen` entries, rounding out the defensive `.get(i)` handling `finalize_stop_sequences` (round 57) and the spec-verifier already use; production batches (all lengths equal) are byte-for-byte unchanged. 1 new unit test. (RIL ISS-077 / TASK-091.)
- **Rejected engine admission no longer leaks `finish_reason_tx` under key 0** — `handle_add_request` inserted the caller's finish-reason oneshot at `seq_id 0` unconditionally when `add_request` rejected a request (empty prompt), and no sequence is ever id 0, so `finalize_finished` never removed it: a permanent oneshot-sender leak, and a hang for a direct engine caller awaiting `finish_reason_rx`. The server layer rejects empty prompts first, so this was direct-engine-only; the fix skips tracking the sender for un-admitted (`seq_id == 0`) requests. 1 regression test. (RIL ISS-076 / TASK-090.)
- **Token-channel drops are now observable (`dropped_tokens_total` metric + warning)** — the engine sends every sampled token into the 64-slot response channel with `try_send` and previously ignored the result, so when a consumer drained slower than generation the channel filled and the token vanished with **no log, no metric, and a permanent gap in the client's stream**. The drop is now logged (`warn!`) and counted in a new `dropped_tokens_total` counter exported on `/metrics`, so silent loss is detectable; `Closed` (receiver dropped — client cancelled) stays a silent no-op. 2 new tests (counter increment + Full-channel drop at engine level). Public API +1 (`EnhancedMetricsCollector::record_dropped_token`). (RIL ISS-074 / TASK-089.)
- **CUDA-Graph batch path now applies the same memory-admission guards as `build_batch` (latent KV corruption + preemption livelock)** — `select_sequences_for_phase` (the `cuda-graph` composition path used whenever CUDA graphs are enabled) was missing four guards the regular `build_batch` path has (RIL ISS-072): (1) the ISS-055/056 **additional-blocks** preemption gate — the graph path compared the full block count, so a running decode holding its whole table re-charged itself for it and got needlessly preempted into a self-preemption livelock under memory pressure; (2) `filter_fully_allocated_prefills` (ISS-042) — a prefill whose KV table could not be fully pre-allocated was admitted and its missing blocks fell back to **block 0**, corrupting a live block owned by another sequence; (3) `admit_decode_sequences` (ISS-052) — a short-table `Decoding` sequence was forwarded and its next token write fell back to block 0; (4) `requeue_uncomposed_prefills` (ISS-041) — a drained-but-uncomposed prefill stayed `Prefilling` in `running` forever with its KV pinned. The `cuda-graph` feature is non-default, so this is latent in default builds but active in `--all-features` CI and any embedder enabling CUDA graphs. 2 new regression tests (short-table decode growth, no-over-preemption of a full-table decode). (RIL ISS-072 / TASK-085.)
- **A single NaN logit can no longer produce an arbitrary token or NaN logprobs (sampling correctness)** — `temperature_sample` and `top_p_sample` guarded only on `max_val` finiteness, but `f32::max` silently ignores NaN, so a NaN among finite logits left `max_val` finite while the softmax exp-sum became NaN → every `cumsum` comparison was false → the sampler fell through to the **tail index `len-1`** (a non-argmax garbage token). `log_softmax` and `renormalized_top_p_logprobs` shared the poisoning (NaN logprobs for finite entries → broken `SampledToken` JSON). All four now sanitise `NaN` → `-inf` before the softmax (NaN contributes `exp(-inf) == 0` probability), so the finite entries recover the correct distribution and the argmax wins; `+inf` is deliberately left for the existing greedy-argmax guard (which strictly picks it). 6 new tests. (RIL ISS-073 / TASK-086.)
- **`/v1/embeddings` now applies the sibling boundary checks (hardening-parity gap)** — the handler validated only that the `input` *array* was non-empty, so `input: [""]` / `["   "]` flowed through to a zero-token embed that sync completions explicitly forbids (`req.prompt.is_empty()` → 400), an input whose token count exceeded the model context forced a full-length embed forward with no `check_context_length` gate (ISS-044 class, bounded only by the 1 MiB body limit), and the element count was uncapped (each element is a full model-dimension vector — the generation siblings bound fan-out via `n ≤ 8` / `best_of ≤ 20`). The handler now rejects empty/whitespace-only elements (400 `invalid_request_error`), gates every input's token count against `max_model_len` (400 `context_length_exceeded`), and caps the element count at 2,048 (the OpenAI embeddings API's documented bound). 9 new unit tests. (RIL ISS-068 / TASK-081.)
- **Batch `create_batch` now bounds the batch size, and the batch worker bounds its seq-id wait (resource-exhaustion hardening)** — the inline `prompts` array was previously unbounded: each prompt is a full engine generation, so one request packed N× work (sync completions is 1 generation/request, `n` ≤ 8) and the rate-limit cost clamp at `100_000` made prompts past the ~1,000-prompt saturation point free (round-59 cost-bypass class). `create_batch` now rejects batches over 10,000 prompts (400 `invalid_request_error`, before persisting — 1/5 of the OpenAI Batch API's documented 50,000-per-file cap, since the whole batch is spawned from one request body). Separately, the batch worker awaited the engine's `seq_id` reply with **no timeout** (matching a stalled engine would hang the job in `InProgress` forever — and `BatchManager` only evicts terminal jobs, so the retention was permanent); the wait is now bounded at 3s (vs. the 1s HTTP streaming bound — batch is background work, leaving headroom for a real engine mid-forward), and a timeout/closed channel fails the item with a clear message and the worker continues. 2 new handler tests (cap accept/reject, reject-before-persist) + 2 new worker tests incl. a stalled-engine regression (fails pre-fix). (RIL ISS-070 / TASK-083.)
- **Chat completions now validates in every dispatch path (hardening-parity gap)** — `validate_chat_request` (model/messages non-empty) ran only in `handle_chat` (the n=1 non-stream path); the streaming and n>1 dispatches (`stream_chat_completion`, `run_n_parallel_chat`, `stream_n_parallel_chat`) never validated, so `messages: []` or `model: ""` with `stream: true` produced a **200 SSE stream of an empty completion** instead of the 400 that non-stream chat and sync completions (which validates the empty prompt pre-dispatch, `completions.rs:1402`) both return. Validation now runs once at the `chat_completions` boundary before the stream/non-stream/n-parallel dispatch. Per-message validation was also missing: empty content is now rejected (the sync empty-prompt contract applied per-message), and the free `role` `String` is now constrained to `{system, user, assistant, tool}` — an unknown/misspelled role was previously **silently dropped by every chat template** (`_ => {}` in `chat_template.rs`), i.e. silent data loss wrapped in a 200 OK. 4 new unit tests + 3 new handler-level streaming regression tests. (RIL ISS-069 / TASK-082.)
- **Batch `create_batch` rejects empty prompt strings, inheriting the sync `/v1/completions` empty-prompt contract** — the batch `Completion` branch passed each `prompt` unguarded to tokenization, so a `prompts: [""]` entry would have reached the worker as a zero-token prefill that the sync endpoint explicitly forbids (`req.prompt.is_empty()` → 400 "prompt is required"). Any empty string in the list now fails the whole batch with `400 invalid_request_error` before the job is persisted. 2 new handler tests. (RIL ISS-067 / TASK-080.)
- **Batch `create_batch` now enforces the same sampling-param boundary validation as chat/completions (hardening-parity gap)** — the batch endpoint accepted `temperature` and `max_tokens` unvalidated: NaN / ±inf / out-of-[0,2] `temperature` was forwarded verbatim into `SamplingParams` by the worker (silently corrupting sampling, the ISS-048 contract; serde_json accepts bare `NaN`/`Infinity` tokens, so the path is HTTP-reachable), and `max_tokens = 0` still emitted one token while a negative value was silently coerced to 100 via `usize::try_from(...).unwrap_or(100)` (the ISS-033 contract). Round 59 hardened the batch endpoint for rate-limit cost + the ISS-044 context-length ceiling; the float/int boundary checks were the remaining parity gap. Both are now rejected with `400 invalid_request_error` **before** the job is persisted, so an invalid batch never spawns a worker over garbage params. 9 new handler tests (boundary pass + NaN / ±inf / negative / out-of-range temperature + zero / negative `max_tokens` rejection, including no-persist). (RIL ISS-065 / TASK-078, CHG-075.)
- **Batch jobs now expire: stored `expires_at` + lazy eviction of terminal jobs** — `BatchManager` previously retained every created job (and its full per-prompt results) in-memory **forever**, while every response synthesized `expires_at = now + 86_400` — a moving target that extended retention on each read and contradicted the documented "results still retrievable until then" contract. `BatchJob` now carries a fixed `expires_at` (`created_at + DEFAULT_BATCH_RETENTION_SECS`), responses report that stored value, and the manager's access points lazily evict **terminal** (`Completed`/`Failed`/`Cancelled`) jobs past their expiry — bounding the in-memory map to ~one retention window with no background sweeper. In-flight (`Pending`/`InProgress`) jobs are never evicted, so a running worker never loses its scope. 7 new tests (manager eviction semantics + response contract). Public API delta +2 (`BatchJob::expires_at`, `BatchJob::is_terminal`). (RIL ISS-066 / TASK-079, DEC-046.)
- **Prefill KV blocks were allocated after the forward, corrupting the cache (fundamental latent correctness bug)** — KV blocks were allocated only in `update()`, *after* the model forward writes KV. For a freshly-admitted prefill (`kv_blocks` empty), the model's `write_prefill_kv` fell back to **block 0** for every position, then `update()` allocated the correct (empty) blocks — so the prompt KV landed in the wrong blocks and decode read garbage. This corrupted **all** inference with a real (KV-writing) model. Latent because `StubModel`'s `passthrough_paged_prefill` ignores the KV cache (never writes) and no real model has been run (OPS-04). Fix: `build_batch` now allocates each newly-admitted sequence's prompt KV blocks (`⌈prompt_len / BLOCK_SIZE⌉`) after the memory/preemption check and before the batch is forwarded, mirroring `update()`'s refcount recording and (under multi-node) block-token chain hashing; decode still grows incrementally in `update()`. New `test_build_batch_preallocates_prefill_kv_blocks` (40-token prompt → 3 distinct blocks) verified to fail pre-fix (got 0 blocks). (RIL ISS-022 / TASK-023, DEC-008.)
- **Partial prefix-cache hit resumed prefill into single-token decode (latent correctness bug)** — `classify_prefill_seq` set `is_prefill = (start == 0)`, so a sequence resuming prefill after a **partial** prefix-cache hit (`num_computed_tokens > 0` with a multi-token suffix) got `is_prefill = false` and `run_layers` dispatched the multi-token suffix to `forward_decode` — which reshapes to `seq_len = 1` and fails / mis-processes any suffix longer than one token. The correct path already existed (`forward_prefill → forward_prefill_continue` when `num_computed_tokens > 0`, reading the cached KV under a rectangular mask) but was unreachable. Fix: `is_prefill = true` for resumes (the composer only handles prefilling sequences). This affects production scenarios with shared prefixes (e.g. system prompts) where a new prompt partially matches a cached prefix with a multi-token remainder. New `test_partial_prefill_resume_keeps_is_prefill_true` (fails pre-fix); also corrected `test_prefill_batch_with_partial_computed`, which had been **pinning the buggy** `is_prefill = false`. (RIL ISS-021 / TASK-022.)
- **Quantized KV-cache used one per-layer scale for per-block quantization (latent correctness bug, opt-in)** — the quantized paged KV cache stored a single scale per layer, but each block is quantized with its own max-derived scale. `write_kv`'s read-modify-write also treated the stored int8 values (held in f32) as raw floats when re-quantizing, so the first-written token got stuck at the quantized extreme (127) and corrupted the scale; and `read_kv` dequantized every block with the *last* block's scale. Empirically, a 20-token prefill into blocks of magnitude 100 / 1 read back `[127, 100, …]` / `[127, 1, …]`. Fix: per-block scales (`scales[layer][block]`), `write_kv` dequantizes the existing block with its own scale before modifying, `read_kv` dequantizes each block with its own scale, and the multi-node block fetch uses the block's scale. KV quantization is off by default, so no default-path impact. New `test_quantized_multi_block_roundtrip_per_block_scale` verified to fail pre-fix. (RIL ISS-020 / TASK-021.)
- **Multi-block KV-cache writes corrupted any prompt longer than one block (critical latent correctness bug)** — two KV-write paths disagreed with `read_kv` (the read path attention uses) about which block holds a token. `read_kv` iterates `block_ids` in block order (`block_ids[b]` holds tokens `[b·block_size, …)`), so the token at `global_pos` lives in `block_ids[global_pos / block_size]`. But `write_prefill_kv` indexed `block_ids[token_idx]` (treating it as one-per-**token**) and `read_decode_kv` used the raw block **index** as the block id. With `BLOCK_SIZE = 16` and a 20-token prefill into non-contiguous blocks `[5, 9]`, a `0..19` round-trip came back `[16,17,18,19,4,5,…,15,0,1,0,0]` — scrambled — and the decode token at position 20 landed in block 1 instead of block 9. Both only coincided with the correct layout when block ids are contiguous from 0 **and** the prompt fits one block, so every existing test (single block, `block_ids=[0]`) passed while **all real multi-block inference was corrupted**. Fix: both paths compute `block_idx = global_pos / block_size` and look up `block_ids[block_idx]`, matching `read_kv`. New `test_multi_block_kv_roundtrip_non_contiguous_blocks` (non-contiguous blocks, value-exact round-trip + decode placement) verified to fail pre-fix. (RIL ISS-019 / TASK-020.)
- **K8s health/readiness probes no longer require API-key auth (operational bug)** — the auth middleware was mounted app-wide with **no path exemption**, so when API keys were configured (the production case) the Helm chart's unauthenticated liveness/readiness probes (`GET /health/live`, `GET /health/ready`) received **401 and the pod never became ready** (restart loop). Verified empirically (with auth configured, `GET /health/live` without a key returned 401). Fix: a narrow `is_health_path` exemption passes `/health`, `/health/live`, `/health/ready`, `/ready` through without auth; `/metrics`, `/debug/*`, and `/shutdown` **stay authenticated** (the exemption is not a wholesale bypass — verified `/v1/models` still returns 401). The guard against an unauthenticated *public* deployment remains the startup refusal to bind non-loopback without keys. New tests `health_probes_are_exempt_from_api_key_auth` (failed pre-fix) and `non_health_endpoints_still_require_auth`. (RIL ISS-018 / TASK-019.)
- **Batched speculative draft generation advanced positions by `len()`, not `last_pos+1` (latent correctness bug)** — `generate_batched_drafts` advanced the per-sequence draft positions with `current_positions[i].push(current_positions[i].len())` — pushing the **count** instead of the next position. For a sequence decoding at position P the draft positions came out `[P, 1, 2, …]` instead of `[P, P+1, P+2, …]`, so the draft model applied RoPE at the wrong positions for every draft past the first, corrupting the proposed drafts. The newer per-seq path already advanced correctly (`last_pos + 1`); the legacy batched path (used when `draft_resolver` is `None`, i.e. `Engine::new_boxed` with a shared draft model) had diverged. Same bug class as the verification fix below, found by auditing the draft-generation side. Fix: advance with `last().copied().unwrap_or(0) + 1`. New `test_batched_draft_positions_advance_from_base` (position-recording draft model) verified to fail pre-fix (`got 1 in [2, 1]`). (RIL ISS-017 / TASK-018.)
- **Speculative-decoding verification used 0-based RoPE positions (latent correctness bug)** — `verify_draft_tokens_logits` built `verify_positions = [0, 1, …, n−1]` for the target-model verification forward pass, but RoPE consumes **absolute** positions (threaded unchanged through `forward_logits → forward_with_paged_kv → run_decoder_layers → LayerCtx.positions → attention`; `num_computed_tokens` is a separate field used only for KV-slot allocation, not a RoPE offset). So for any sequence past position 0 — i.e. **every speculative step after prefill** — the target model computed logits with RoPE at the wrong positions, corrupting the accept/reject decisions and the emitted tokens. The empty-drafts path already passed `batch.positions[i]` correctly; the drafts path was inconsistent with it. Fix: start `verify_positions` at the first input token's true position (`batch.positions[i][0]`). New `test_verification_uses_true_sequence_positions` (a position-recording mock) asserts every verification position is ≥ 2 after a 2-token prefill; verified to fail pre-fix (`got 0 in [0,1,2,3]`). The existing spec_dispatch tests missed this because their mock models ignore positions. (RIL ISS-016 / TASK-017.)
- **FP8 E4M3 KV-cache quantizer corrected (8x dequant error + underflow saturation)** — two bugs, both hidden by tests that never checked values. (1) `fp8_e4m3_to_float16` computed the exponent as `biased_exp − 7 − 3`, so **every dequantized value was 8× too small** (1.0 → 0.125, −3.0 → −0.375) — any model on an FP8 KV cache would have had all KV values scaled by ⅛, corrupting attention. (2) Encode underflow: values below the smallest normal (`|x| < 2⁻⁶`) produced a negative `biased_exp` whose `as u8` cast wrapped to a huge value and tripped the overflow check, so **tiny values mapped to the largest finite FP8** (0.001 → ~44). Fixes: decode exponent `= biased_exp − 7`; encode flushes underflow to zero, corrects the overflow threshold to `biased_exp > 14`, and handles the mantissa round-up carry. The round-trip test asserted **shape only** and the small-values test had a **vacuous** always-true clause; both now check actual values (round-trip within ~15% relative error, sub-normals flush to ~0) and were verified to fail pre-fix. FP8 KV cache is not yet wired into production (re-exported only), so no deployed impact, but the public primitive is now correct (worst normal-range round-trip error 4%). (RIL ISS-015 / TASK-015.)
- **RoPE rotation signs corrected to the standard convention (latent correctness bug)** — `apply_rope_with_inv_freq` computed `x1' = x1·cos + x2·sin`, `x2' = x2·cos − x1·sin`, which is the **inverse** rotation `R(−θ)`. The standard RoPE (Su et al. 2021) and HuggingFace `apply_rotary_pos_emb` (used by Llama / Qwen / Mistral) require `x1' = x1·cos − x2·sin`, `x2' = x2·cos + x1·sin`. Because both q and k pass through the same function, the relative-position term came out as `R(m−n)` instead of `R(n−m)` — the positional encoding's **direction was reversed for every RoPE model**. The cos/sin cache formula was already correct; only the combination signs were wrong. Latent for the same reason as the GQA bug: RoPE tests asserted only shape / determinism / positional-sensitivity / finiteness, never the rotation values against a reference, and there is no real-checkpoint validation (OPS-04 deferred). Fix flips the two sin-term signs; new `test_apply_rope_matches_standard_rotation_convention` pins the exact HuggingFace reference output and was verified to fail pre-fix (output confirmed to match the reference bit-for-bit after the fix). (RIL ISS-012 / TASK-012.)
- **MRoPE now rotates both halves of each section (latent correctness bug)** — `apply_rope_section` computed the first half correctly (`x1·cos − x2·sin`) but emitted the second half **unchanged**, applying only half the rotation and breaking the relative-position property for Qwen3.5-VL multimodal sections. Fix computes the second half (`x2·cos + x1·sin`); new `test_mrope_rotates_both_halves_standard_convention` (a single size-4 section ≡ standard RoPE) pins the reference output and was verified to fail pre-fix. (RIL ISS-013 / TASK-013.)
- **GQA attention now uses correct blocked head grouping (latent correctness bug)** — `expand_kv` (the shared helper behind `RopeGqaAttention`, i.e. the production attention path for Llama / Mistral / Qwen3) expanded KV heads with `Tensor::repeat`, which **tiles** (`[K0, K1, K0, K1, …]`). Grouped-query attention requires **blocked** (repeat-interleave) grouping `[K0, K0, …, K1, K1, …]` so query head `h` attends to KV head `⌊h / group_size⌋`; the tiled layout instead paired query head `h` with KV head `h % num_kv_heads` — the **wrong KV head for every group past the first**, silently corrupting attention output for all GQA models. The bug was latent because every `expand_kv` test asserted only the output *shape*, never the head *ordering*, and the engine has no real-checkpoint end-to-end validation (OPS-04 deferred). The correct blocked idiom already existed in `gemma4/attention/kernels.rs`; the shared util + flash paths had diverged. Fixed all three sites — `util::expand_kv` (`[batch,seq,heads,dim]`) and `flash_attention_v3` `GqaFlashAttention`/`MqaFlashAttention` `expand_kv` (`[batch,heads,seq,dim]`) — via the reshape→`broadcast_as`→contiguous→reshape repeat-interleave idiom, then `narrow` to the exact query-head count. 4 new regression tests, all verified to **fail on the pre-fix tiled code**: a direct ordering test (`[0,0,0,0,1,1,1,1]` not `[0,1,0,1,0,1,0,1]`), an end-to-end single-token `paged_attention` test (query heads {0,1} must output V0, {2,3} must output V1), and two flash-path ordering tests. (RIL ISS-010 / TASK-010.)
- **Gated DeltaNet `repeat_kv_heads` now uses blocked grouping (same bug class)** — a follow-up sweep of the ISS-010 tile-vs-blocked defect found a fourth site: the Gated DeltaNet (Mamba / SSM hybrid) `repeat_kv_heads` expanded K/Q heads to `num_v_heads` with `Tensor::repeat` (tiled). The DeltaNet recurrence is per-head, so V head `v` shares K/Q head `⌊v / group⌋`; the tiled layout paired V head `v` with the wrong K/Q head (`v % num_k_heads`). HuggingFace's canonical `repeat_kv` (used by Qwen3-Next `gated_delta_net`) is blocked. Fixed with the same repeat-interleave idiom; new `test_repeat_kv_heads_blocked_grouping` verified to fail pre-fix. (RIL ISS-011 / TASK-011.)
- **Seeded sampling now draws a per-step stream, not a frozen threshold** — `random_f32_seeded` re-seeded a fresh `StdRng` with the bare request seed on **every** decode step and read its first value, so a seeded generation drew the **same** random threshold at every step (per-step quantile lock): the nucleus / temperature cutoff froze and cross-step independence collapsed, producing degenerate repetitive output and contradicting OpenAI's seed semantic (reproducible *yet* per-step-varied). The per-step seed is now derived from `(seed, step)` via a fixed `SplitMix64` mix, where `step` is the already-generated seen-set length (deterministic for a given seed + prompt), so step `n` draws a distinct, reproducible position of the stream — same seed + same prompt ⇒ same output, distinct steps ⇒ independent draws. The unseeded thread-RNG path is unchanged. 6 new deterministic regression tests pin both halves of the contract; two pre-existing tests that asserted a fragile probabilistic "different seeds ⇒ different tokens" on a peaked distribution were hardened to assert the deterministic guarantee (distinct thresholds for distinct seeds). (RIL ISS-007 / TASK-007.)
- **`repeat_penalty` documentation corrected to per-distinct (not frequency-style)** — several doc comments falsely described `apply_repeat_penalty` as "frequency-style / proportional to occurrence count / divided once per occurrence (a token seen 3 times gets divided 3 times)". The implementation dedupes via a `HashSet` and applies the multiplicative, sign-aware adjustment **once per distinct token** (llama.cpp / HuggingFace repetition-penalty style). Corrected the `SamplingParams::presence_penalty` field doc, the server `frequency_penalty` honoring notes, and a test comment; explicitly noted that OpenAI's `frequency_penalty` is genuinely per-occurrence, so the engine's per-distinct `repeat_penalty` is an approximation (direction correct, count-scaling not). Docs-only, no behavior change. (RIL ISS-008 / TASK-008.)
- **Sampling no longer panics on empty logits rows** — the top-k mask blocks in `sample_batch` / `sample_one_with_params` and the `top_logprobs` ranking called `select_nth_unstable_by(n − 1)` on a potentially empty slice: a model returning an empty logits row (reaching the engine via `extract_per_seq_logits`) panicked the inference loop in both debug (subtraction overflow) and release (index out of bounds) — even though every other pipeline stage (greedy, temperature, top-p, argmax) degrades to greedy on empty input. The mask and ranking steps are now skipped when there is no distribution, restoring the module's defensive empty-input contract. 3 new unit tests (top-k + empty, top_logprobs + empty, batch row + empty).
- **`get_memory_pressure` no longer divides by zero on zero-block configs** — a degenerate config with zero KV blocks produced `NaN` pressure (0/0), poisoning scheduling decisions (preemption never triggered). Now reports maximum pressure (1.0) so the scheduler preempts; pinned by a new unit test. Also restored the clippy gate broken by the temperature-fix commit (a deny-tier `uninlined_format_args` error and `cast_lossless` warnings in the new statistical test).
- **Speculative KV rollback and preemption now honor the eviction refcount contract** — `MemoryManager::rollback` (and the no-caller legacy `MemoryManager::execute_preemption`) called `free`, which bypasses the eviction policy.
